> inside this folder there is a test that you are going to do to get to know Railcon,
  first read the contents of the folder to understand the scope

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 10 files, listed 1 directory (ctrl+o to expand)

● I read the whole folder. It is a front-end test for Natura Wellness (a
  fictional brand), in Vite + React + TypeScript, with two parts of about 30
  minutes each. I have not written any code yet.

  Part A: "Manage my subscription" card

  The file is SubscriptionCard.tsx (src/part-a/SubscriptionCard.tsx), currently
  just a placeholder. There is no design, so the decisions are mine. The API
  lives in src/api/, with getSubscription() and skipNextShipment().

  Requirements:
  - Quick view: show which products are coming, when the charge will be and the
    amount.
  - Skip a shipment: the customer skips the next shipment and the charge moves
    to the following cycle.
  - Confirmation: ask for confirmation before skipping, to avoid support
    tickets.
  - Paused or cancelled: look different from active and have no skip button.
  - Mobile first: 375px wide.
  - Errors and slowness: the API has a latency of 0.4 to 2.5s and returns 409
    when the subscription changed elsewhere. The customer must always know what
    happened and what to do.

  API details that affect the implementation:
  - skipNextShipment requires passing the loaded updatedAt as expectedUpdatedAt.
  - On a 409, the right thing is to reload the subscription and offer a new
    attempt.
  - nextChargeAt and nextChargeTotalCents are null outside the active state.
  - imageUrl can be null, so I need a placeholder.
  - There are 3 status fixtures and one 500-error fixture. The URL
    ?fixture=active|paused|cancelled|error picks the case and &conflict=always
    forces the 409.
  - The screen opens at /?part=a&fixture=active.

  Part B: buy box

  The file is BuyBox.tsx (src/part-b/BuyBox.tsx), also just a placeholder. I
  need to reproduce buy-box.png (public/reference/buy-box.png), which is a 2x
  screenshot of a 1200px window, so the measurements in the image are worth
  half in CSS.

  What the image shows:
  - Background and photo: cream background, with the product photo in a rounded
    card on the left.
  - Right column header: eyebrow "VITAMINS", stars with 4.8 and a reviews link,
    title "Vitamin D" in dark-green Fraunces and a subtitle.
  - Purchase options: two, "One-time" ($29.95) and "Subscribe & save 15%". The
    second is selected, with a green border, greenish background, a "BEST
    VALUE" badge, price $25.46 with $29.95 struck through, a "Delivered every
    30 days" select and two items with a check.
  - Cart: quantity selector and an "Add to cart · $25.46" button.
  - Footer: three trust badges and the legal text.

  The texts are in copy.ts (src/part-b/copy.ts), the Fraunces and Inter fonts
  are already in index.html, and the photo is /product.jpg. Selecting the
  option and the quantity must work. The price on the button must follow the
  option and the quantity. Add to cart does not need to do anything.

  Delivery

  The test asks for a public GitHub repository, a live link (Vercel or
  StackBlitz) and the link to the conversation with the AI. They evaluate how I
  drive and check the work, not just the result.

  Where do you want to start: Part A, Part B, or both in sequence? If it is A,
  do you want me to already run npm install and start the development server?

● I did not write code. I read the measurements on the 2000px-wide preview, so
  I converted each one with CSS px = px read × 0.6. The 1200px window (2400px
  in the original 2x) ends up 780px tall.

  Limit of this reading: the hex colors and font sizes are visual estimates. I
  did not sample pixels. The text widths gave me the font sizes, with a ±1px
  margin. Before coding I can run a script that samples the exact hex values
  from the image, so I do not have to estimate colors.

  1. Layout description

  Page and columns

  - The background is cream, about #FAF7F2.
  - The content spans from x=64 to x=1136, with a 64px margin on both sides.
    The top of the content is at y≈56.
  - There are two columns aligned at the top, with 64px between them.
    - The left one is 540px and is the image card.
    - The right one is 468px and starts at x=668.

  Left column: photo card

  - It is a 540×540 square, from y=56 to y=595.
  - The background is white, the border is 1px light beige (#EDE6DA) and the
    radius is about 24px.
  - The bottle is centered. I do not know whether the photo fills the card or
    has padding. I need to open product.jpg to see the proportion and
    background before deciding on object-fit.

  Right column, top to bottom

  Element: Eyebrow "VITAMINS"
  Position (center Y, in CSS px): ≈63
  Estimate: Inter ≈13px, weight 500, uppercase, letter-spacing ≈0.12em, color
  #6B7A6F (greenish gray)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Element: Stars + rating
  Position (center Y, in CSS px): ≈93
  Estimate: 5 gold stars (#D4A03A), 18px step between them, ≈14–16px each.
  "4.8" at 15px, weight 600, #2B2B2B. "2,316 reviews" at 15px, underlined,
  #6B7A6F, with ≈8px gap
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Element: Title "Vitamin D"
  Position (center Y, in CSS px): baseline ≈154
  Estimate: Fraunces, capital letter height ≈32px, which gives a font of
  ≈46–48px. Weight ≈600, dark green #1B4332, slightly negative letter-spacing
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Element: Subtitle
  Position (center Y, in CSS px): 2 lines, line height ≈25px
  Estimate: Inter ≈16px, weight 400, #4A4F4B, wraps at "a / day," because of
  the column width (468px)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Element: Divider
  Position (center Y, in CSS px): y≈252
  Estimate: 1px, #E8E2D6, full column width

  Option cards

  The gap between the two cards is ≈12px. The first card starts 24px below the
  divider. Both are 468px wide with a radius of ≈14px.

  One-time purchase: y=277–336, height ≈60px.
  - The background is white and the border is 1px #E6DFD2.
  - The horizontal padding is ≈20px.
  - The radio is ≈20px in diameter, with a gray border (#A9ADA8) of ≈1.5–2px.
    The distance to the label is ≈12px.
  - The label is Inter ≈17px, weight 600, dark color.
  - The price is on the right, at ≈20px, weight 600/700.

  Subscribe & save (selected): y=349–529, height ≈180px.
  - The border is 2px #1B4332 and the background is pale green, about #F3F5F1.
  - The padding is ≈20px.
  - The selected radio has a green ring and an inner dot of ≈10px.
  - The header is a center-aligned row: radio, label (same style as the other),
    badge and price on the right.
  - The "BEST VALUE" badge is a pill of ≈95×20px, pale-gold background #F6E7B9,
    text #8A6A12, ≈12px, weight 700, uppercase and letter-spacing ≈0.06em. The
    gap from the label is ≈12–14px.
  - The price on the right has two right-aligned lines. "$25.46" is at ≈20px,
    weight 600/700. "$29.95" is struck through, at ≈14px, #7A7F7A.
  - Below comes the "Delivered every" + select row, with the content indented
    ≈52px, which is padding 20 + radio 20 + gap 12.
    - The select measures ≈115×32px, with a white background, 1px #D9D3C7
      border, ≈8px radius and ≈12px left padding.
    - The "30 days" text is ≈15px, weight 500, and the arrow is a small chevron
      on the right (≈8×5px).
    - The "Delivered every" label is ≈15px, color #555, with ≈10px gap to the
      select.
  - The two perks sit below, with a ≈25px step between the lines.
    - The check icon is dark green, in a ≈16px box, with ≈8px gap to the text.
    - The text is ≈15px, #3E4440.

  Purchase row

  Starts 24px below the card, at y=553–608, height ≈55–56px.
  - The stepper is ≈128px wide, white background, 1px #E0D9CC border and ≈12px
    radius.
    - It has "−", "1" and "+" distributed. The number is ≈17px, weight 500/600.

  - The gap to the CTA is ≈12px.
  - The CTA takes up the rest (≈328px), with #1B4332 background and ≈12px

  Trust badges

  2. Implementation plan

  Files:
  - src/styles/tokens.css: CSS variables (colors, radii, fonts). This is what

  State:
  - option: "oneTime" | "subscribe", with initial value subscribe.
  - quantity: number with a minimum of 1.
  - frequency: the select's value.
  - The total is derived: option price × quantity. It is not kept in state.

  Build order:
  1. Read product.jpg, main.tsx and index.html.
  2. Create tokens.css and format.ts.
  3. Build the skeleton of the two columns, with the photo card and the header
     (eyebrow, rating, title, subtitle and divider).
  4. Build the option cards, first the selected one and then the unselected
     variation.

     print.

     instead of using my estimates.

  starting at (84, 76), the bottle's left edge lands at x≈233 and the top at

  ≈500px, square, with object-fit: contain. The card needs overflow: hidden to
  respect the 24px radius. The card background needs to be exactly the white of
  the photo, otherwise a seam appears. I will check this in the color
  sampling, because the JPEG may have a white slightly off #FFFFFF.

  update the plan

● Point 1: the fine print

  The text comes ready-made from copy.ts and is a fixed string:

  ▎ "Your subscription renews every 30 days at $25.46 until you cancel. Skip or
  ▎ cancel anytime from your account."

  The image only shows the state with the subscription selected, at 30 days,
  quantity 1. That leaves three situations unanswered:
  - 60 or 90-day frequency: the select offers those options, but the text
    would keep saying "30 days".
  - One-time purchase selected: the text about the subscription renewal would
    keep appearing, although it does not apply.
  - More than 1 quantity: the "$25.46" is the unit price. The button shows the
    total, and the text does not.

  There are three paths:
  - A. Literal (recommended): the text always appears, exactly as in
    copy.ts. It is the most faithful to "do not improvise", but it ends up
    inconsistent in those cases.
  - B. Only with subscription: the text stays the same, but disappears when
    "One-time" is selected. It solves the second case, but it is a behavior
    the image does not show.
  - C. Dynamic: the text starts using the chosen frequency and price. It would
    be the most correct, but it changes the copy.ts text and departs from the
    design.

  I will go with A and record the limitation in the README. If you prefer B or
  C, tell me before step 7.

  Point 3: mobile first

  - Part A: yes, it is mobile first. The brief asks for 375px first.
  - Part B: it is not mobile first by nature. The reference is desktop at
    1200px, and fidelity to it only holds at that width.

  I understood "only one column" as the minimal rule I proposed: two columns on
  desktop, as in the image, and stacked into one column on mobile. If you meant
  one column at all widths, that would contradict the image, so let me know.

  I will write the CSS with the mobile-first technique: the base style is one
  column and an @media (min-width: 768px) turns on the two columns. The values
  measured from the image apply from that breakpoint. The mobile values (page
  padding, full-width square photo) will be my choices, because the design does
  not define them.

  Updated plan

  Closed decisions
  - Quantity: minimum 1, maximum 99.
  - Mobile: one column below 768px, written mobile first. Mobile values are my
    choices and are flagged.
  - Fine print: option A (literal), pending confirmation.
  - Photo: ≈20px of padding in the card, object-fit: contain, overflow: hidden.

  1. Read main.tsx and index.html.
  2. Sample the exact colors from buy-box.png, including the card's white

● Only two small questions remain, both a consequence of choosing path C. I
  already put a default for each in the plan. If you agree, just say "you can
  start".

  1. Price in the fine print with quantity greater than 1: the "$25.46" is the
     unit price. Since the text says "renews at $X", the correct value would be
     the total (price × quantity), the same as the button. Default: total. With
     quantity 1 it is identical to the image.
  2. Fine print with "One-time" selected: the text talks about renewal, so it
     does not apply. Default: it disappears. The image does not show this
     state, so it is my choice. If you prefer the text always visible, just say
     so.

  Complete plan

  Closed decisions

  - Quantity: minimum 1, maximum 99.
  - Responsive: mobile-first CSS. The base style is one column, and the
    reference's two columns apply from 768px. The mobile values (page padding,
    full-width square photo) are my choices, because the design does not define
    them.
  - Fine print (path C):
    - copy.ts will not be edited.
    - The text is derived from copy.finePrint. The code replaces "30 days" with
      the selected frequency and "$25.46" with the formatted total.
    - Those two pieces are read from copy.ts itself (first option of the select
      and subscribe.priceCents), so no text is left written in the code.
  - Photo: 540×540 card with ≈20px of padding, image in object-fit: contain and
    overflow: hidden to respect the radius.

    from the image. Part A will reuse it.

  - quantity: from 1 to 99.

  Constants at the top of BuyBox.tsx

  - ONE_TIME_PRICE_CENTS = 2995
  - DISCOUNT_PERCENT = 15
  - SUBSCRIBE_PRICE_CENTS, computed with Math.round(ONE_TIME_PRICE_CENTS * (100
    - DISCOUNT_PERCENT) / 100). The result is 2546, the same as the image.
  - FREQUENCY_OPTIONS = [30, 60, 90], in days
  - MIN_QUANTITY = 1 and MAX_QUANTITY = 99

  Fixed texts (transcribed from the image, written in the component)

  - Header: "Vitamins", "4.8", "2,316 reviews", "Vitamin D" and "2,000 IU (50
    mcg) of vitamin D per softgel. One softgel a day, with food."
  - Options: "One-time purchase", "Best value" (shown in uppercase by CSS) and
    "Delivered every".
  - Perks: "Skip, pause, or cancel anytime" and "Free shipping on every order".
  - Purchase: "Add to cart".
  - Badges: "Third-party tested", "Non-GMO" and "Made in the USA".
  - Fixed pieces of the fine print: "Your subscription renews every", "until you
    cancel. Skip or cancel anytime from your account."
  - Accessibility: the photo's alt and the stepper's aria-label. They are not in
    the image, so they are minimal and functional.

  Derived texts (never fixed in the JSX)

  │ days", "90 days")                  │ ${n} days                          │

  ├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Quantity shown in the stepper      │ quantity state                     │
  └────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘

  The fine print uses the total, the same as the button. With quantity 1 it is
  identical to the image. The full text is assembled in a template with the two
  variable parts and the fixed ones above.

  State

  - option: "oneTime" | "subscribe", with initial value subscribe.
  - quantity: from 1 to 99.
  - frequencyDays: with initial value 30.
  - Derived, without state: the option's unit price, the total and the fine
    print text.

  Files

  - src/styles/tokens.css: colors, radii and fonts, with the sampled hex values.
  - src/part-b/BuyBox.tsx and BuyBox.module.css: constants, state and layout.
  - src/part-b/PurchaseOption.tsx: option card. Receives label, price and extra
    content by props, with no text of its own.
  - src/part-b/QuantityStepper.tsx: the stepper, with value, minimum and maximum
    by props.
  - src/part-b/icons.tsx: Star, Check, CheckCircle, Chevron, Minus, Plus and
    radio, in inline SVG.
  - src/part-b/format.ts: only formatPrice(cents).
  - copy.ts stays in the repository, but is not imported.

  1. Read main.tsx and index.html.
  2. Sample the exact colors from buy-box.png with a script outside the project.
  3. Create tokens.css and format.ts.
  4. Build the skeleton: one column in the base and two from 768px, plus the

     header.
  5. Build the option cards, with the select and the perks.
  6. Build the stepper, the CTA with a derived total, the badges and the
     derived and conditional fine print.
  7. Verify at 1200px with 2x scale, with a diff against the reference and
     deviations of 1–2px. Also test the option change, the three frequencies
     (the fine print must change and disappear on one-time purchase), the 1 and
     99 quantity limits, and at 375px.

> show me the complete implementation plan again

● Complete implementation plan: BuyBox (Part B)

  Closed decisions

  - Stack: React + TypeScript, CSS Modules and inline SVG, no UI libraries.
  - Measurements: CSS px = px of the 2x image ÷ 2.
  - Texts: copy.ts is not imported. The texts are transcribed from the image and
    written in the component.
  - Variable values: they become constants at the top of BuyBox.tsx, and
    everything that depends on them is derived.
  - Responsive: mobile-first CSS.
    - The base style is one column, and the two columns apply from 768px.
    - The mobile values (page padding, full-width square photo) are my choices,
      because the design does not define them.
  - Photo: 540×540 card with ≈20px of padding, image in object-fit: contain and
    overflow: hidden to respect the 24px radius. I measured this on
    product.jpg (1024×1024, white background).
  - Quantity: minimum 1, maximum 99.
  - Fine print: reflects the chosen frequency, uses the total (price ×
    quantity) and disappears when one-time purchase is selected.

  Constants at the top of BuyBox.tsx

  - ONE_TIME_PRICE_CENTS = 2995
  - DISCOUNT_PERCENT = 15
  - SUBSCRIBE_PRICE_CENTS, computed with Math.round(ONE_TIME_PRICE_CENTS * (100
    - DISCOUNT_PERCENT) / 100), which gives 2546
  - FREQUENCY_OPTIONS = [30, 60, 90]
  - MIN_QUANTITY = 1 and MAX_QUANTITY = 99

  Fixed and derived texts

  Fixed (transcribed from the image):
  - Header: "Vitamins", "4.8", "2,316 reviews", "Vitamin D" and "2,000 IU (50
    mcg) of vitamin D per softgel. One softgel a day, with food."
  - Options: "One-time purchase", "Best value" (uppercase via CSS) and
    "Delivered every".
  - Perks: "Skip, pause, or cancel anytime" and "Free shipping on every order".
  - Purchase: "Add to cart".
  - Badges: "Third-party tested", "Non-GMO" and "Made in the USA".
  - Fixed pieces of the fine print: "Your subscription renews every" and "until
    you cancel. Skip or cancel anytime from your account."
  - Accessibility: the photo's alt and the stepper's aria-label, minimal and
    functional.

  Derived (never fixed in the JSX):

  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤

  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤

  │ Select options ("30 days", "60       │ FREQUENCY_OPTIONS, as ${n}       │
  │ days", "90 days")                    │ days                             │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Button total in "Add to cart · $X"   │ selected option's price ×        │

  State

  - option: "oneTime" | "subscribe", with initial value subscribe.
  - quantity: from 1 to 99.
  - frequencyDays: with initial value 30.
  - Derived, without state: the option's unit price, the total and the fine
    print text.

  Files

  - src/styles/tokens.css: colors, radii and fonts, with the sampled hex values.
    Part A will reuse it.
  - src/part-b/BuyBox.tsx and BuyBox.module.css: constants, state and layout.
  - src/part-b/PurchaseOption.tsx: option card, with a hidden native input
    type="radio" and an SVG radio. Receives label, price and extra content by
    props.
  - src/part-b/QuantityStepper.tsx: the stepper, with value, minimum and maximum
    by props.
  - src/part-b/icons.tsx: Star, Check, CheckCircle, Chevron, Minus, Plus and
    radio, in inline SVG.
  - src/part-b/format.ts: only formatPrice(cents).
  - copy.ts stays in the repository, but is not imported.

  Reference measurements (CSS px)

  The final hex values come from the sampling in step 2. The estimates below
  serve as a starting point and have a ±1px margin.

  Page and columns
  - The background is cream, about #FAF7F2.
  - The content spans from x=64 to x=1136, with the top at y=56. The columns are
    540px and 468px, with 64px between them.

  Left column
  - Photo card of 540×540, 1px #EDE6DA border, ≈24px radius, white background.

  Right column
  - Eyebrow: Inter ≈13px, weight 500, uppercase, letter-spacing ≈0.12em, #6B7A6F.
  - Rating: 5 gold stars (#D4A03A), 18px step. "4.8" at 15px,
    weight 600. "2,316 reviews" at 15px, underlined, #6B7A6F.
  - Title: Fraunces ≈46–48px, weight ≈600, #1B4332, slightly negative
    letter-spacing.
  - Subtitle: Inter ≈16px, line height ≈25px, #4A4F4B.
  - Divider: 1px, #E8E2D6, at y≈252.
  - Option cards: 468px wide, ≈12px gap, ≈14px radius, ≈20px padding. They start
    24px below the divider.
    - One-time: height ≈60px, white background, 1px #E6DFD2 border. ≈20px radio
      with gray border #A9ADA8. Label in Inter ≈17px, weight 600. Price at ≈20px,
      weight 600/700.
    - Subscribe (selected): height ≈180px, 2px #1B4332 border, #F3F5F1
      background. The radio has a green ring and a ≈10px dot.
    - Badge: ≈95×20px pill, #F6E7B9 background, #8A6A12 text, ≈12px, weight 700,
      letter-spacing ≈0.06em.
    - Price and struck-through: "$25.46" at ≈20px, and "$29.95" struck through at
      ≈14px, #7A7F7A, right-aligned.
    - Select: ≈115×32px, 1px #D9D3C7 border, ≈8px radius, ≈12px left padding.
      Text at ≈15px, weight 500, with a ≈8×5px chevron. The row content is
      indented ≈52px.
    - Perks: ≈25px step, green check in a ≈16px box, ≈8px gap, text at ≈15px,
      #3E4440.
  - Purchase row: starts 24px below the card, with a height of ≈55–56px.
    - Stepper: ≈128px, white background, 1px #E0D9CC border, ≈12px radius. The
      number is at ≈17px, weight 500/600.
    - CTA: takes the rest (≈328px), with a 12px gap from the stepper, #1B4332
      background, ≈12px radius. White text at ≈17–18px, weight 600. The "·" has
      lower opacity and ≈8px of breathing room.
  - Badges: ≈28px below the stepper. Circle-with-check icon of ≈15px, text at
    ≈14px, weight 500, #4A4F4B. ≈8px gap between icon and text and ≈24px between
    badges.
  - Fine print: ≈13px, line height ≈18px, #7A817B.

  Build order

  1. Reading: read main.tsx and index.html, to know where to import
     tokens.css.
  2. Colors: sample the exact colors from buy-box.png with a script outside the
     project, in the center of flat areas.
     - The points include page background, border and background of the photo
       card, borders and backgrounds of the two options, texts, green of the
       title and the button, gold of the stars, badge, stepper, select, divider
       and fine print.
     - They also include the card's white against the JPEG's white and the green
       of the cap
       and of the label, as a cross-check of the brand green.
     - If any point is ambiguous, I will say so instead of guessing.
  3. Base: create tokens.css with the measured hex values and format.ts.
  4. Skeleton: build one column in the base and two from 768px, with the photo
     card (540×540, ≈20px of padding, contain) and the header (eyebrow,
     rating, title, subtitle and divider).
  5. Options: build the option cards, selected and unselected, with the select
     row and the perks.
  6. Purchase and footer: build the stepper, the CTA with a derived total, the
     badges and the derived and conditional fine print.
  7. Verification:
     - Render at 1200px with 2x scale and compare with the reference side by
       side and with a diff, with deviations of 1–2px. The bottle's position
       must land at ≈(233, 133).
     - Test the option change, the three frequencies (the fine print changes
       and disappears on one-time purchase) and the 1 and 99 quantity limits.
     - Check at 375px, to confirm nothing breaks.

● Part B is built and matches the reference well. I only checked the
  interactive states through the code, I did not click in the browser.

  Result

  - Desktop (1200px, 2x): I compared with buy-box.png by pixel diff and the
    average difference is 1.49 out of 255, with 0.98% of the pixels differing
    by more than 40.
    - The bottle, the cards, the button and the badges land within ≤2px of the
      reference.
    - The title matches the reference within 1px.
  - Mobile (375px): one column, a square full-width photo and no horizontal
    scroll. Only at that width the "BEST VALUE" badge drops to its own line
    under the label, because the row did not fit. The desktop did not change
    with this adjustment.
  - Typecheck: passes.

  What changed relative to the plan

  - Colors: they came from sampling the PNG and replaced the estimates. The
    brand green is #17402F and the dark text is #1C2420.
  - Fonts: my estimates were ≈6% larger. The final values are 16px for the
    labels, 14px for secondary texts, 18px for the prices and 44px for the
    title, at weight 500.
  - Subtitle: I limited it to 440px of width to wrap at "a / day," like in the
    reference. That value is estimated.
  - Stepper's minus button: it is not dimmed at quantity 1, because the
    reference shows it in full color.
  - Subscription card with "One-time" selected: the select and the perks stay
    visible. The image does not show that state and I preferred not to invent a
    behavior.

  Not verified

  I did not click on the page. The option change, the three frequencies, the
  fine print (changes with the days and disappears on one-time purchase) and
  the 1 and 99 quantity limits were only checked by reading the code and by the
  typecheck. The environment had no click automation tool. It is worth testing
  those interactions in the browser.

  Files

  - New: src/styles/tokens.css, src/part-b/format.ts, icons.tsx,
    PurchaseOption.tsx, QuantityStepper.tsx and BuyBox.module.css.
  - Changed: BuyBox.tsx and main.tsx, which now imports tokens.css.
  - tokens.css also zeroes the body margin and sets the cream background. That
    applies to Part A too.

  The development server (port 5199) is still running in the background. The
  comparison files stayed in the session's temporary folder, outside the
  project. Can I move on to Part A reusing tokens.css?

● I reread the files before building the plan.

  Read 4 files (ctrl+o to expand)

● I reread INSTRUCTIONS.md (Part A) and the three files in src/api/. Nothing
  changed since the first reading. Below is the plan, without code.

  API facts that shape the plan

  - skipNextShipment: requires the loaded updatedAt. The mock returns 409 when it
    does not match. With &conflict=always, every skip gives 409, even after
    reloading. Without that parameter, 409 happens in ≈1 of 3 attempts.
  - Actually skipping: adds intervalDays to nextChargeAt, and the same
    calculations serve for the date preview in the dialog.
  - Errors: getSubscription gives 500 on fixture=error and 404 with an unknown
    fixture. The skip gives 400 if the status is not active and 404 if the id
    does not exist.
  - Values: nextChargeTotalCents already comes with the discount. With the
    fixtures' items, the subtotal is $119.85 and the total, $101.87.
  - Image: the Multivitamin has imageUrl: null.

  1. States and visual differentiation

  ┌─────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │    State    │                       What appears                        │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ Skeleton with the same shape as the card (latency goes    │
  │ Loading     │ from 0.4 to 2.5s). Region with aria-busy and a "Loading   │
  │             │ your subscription…" text only for screen readers          │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ Message with role="alert" and a Try again button, which   │
  │ Error on    │ goes back to the skeleton. The text changes: 404 becomes  │
  │ load        │ "We couldn't find your subscription", and 500 or network  │
  │             │ become "Something went wrong on our end"                  │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Active      │ Green "Active" pill with an icon, a highlight block with  │
  │             │ the next charge and the skip button                       │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ Amber "Paused" pill (reuses Part B's badge colors), a     │
  │ Paused      │ "Paused until {pausedUntil}" block and the phrase "No     │
  │             │ charges while paused". The products appear without prices │
  │             │ and there is no skip button (it does not go in the DOM)   │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ Gray "Cancelled" pill and a "Cancelled on {cancelledAt}"  │
  │ Cancelled   │ block with "You won't be charged again". Grayscale        │
  │             │ thumbnails, no prices and no skip                         │
  └─────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The difference between the states never depends only on color: each one has
  its own text and icon.

  2. Content of the active subscription

  - Header: "Manage my subscription" title in Fraunces and the status pill.
  - "Next charge" block: long date ("Fri, Nov 6") with "in 6 days" and the amount
    large. Below, "Delivered every 30 days".
  - Product list: 56px thumbnail, name, "Qty 2" and the line price (before the
    discount). A missing image becomes a placeholder with the product's initial
    in a tinted square.
  - Summary: Subtotal, "Subscribe & save 15%" and Total. The discount is
    computed as subtotal − nextChargeTotalCents, so the sum always closes with
    the value the API charges.

  3. Skip flow

  1. Button: "Skip next shipment" in a secondary style (outline), to reduce
     accidental taps.
  2. Confirmation dialog: title "Skip your next shipment?". The text shows the
     change: "Your {amount} charge on Nov 6 will move to Dec 6". It also says
     the subscription is not cancelled. The buttons are Yes, skip and Keep my
     shipment, and the initial focus goes to the safe button, "Keep".
  3. In progress: the reducer only accepts the send action when it is in
     "confirming", so a second click triggers nothing. The button uses
     aria-disabled instead of disabled, so it does not lose focus, and shows
     "Skipping…". While the call is in flight the dialog does not close (neither
     with Esc nor on the backdrop), because closing would not cancel the
     request.
  4. Success: the dialog closes, the card switches to the data returned by the
     server (with the new updatedAt) and a notice appears with role="status":
     "Shipment skipped. Your next charge of $101.87 is now Dec 6 (was Nov 6)."

  4. Skip errors

  Error: 409
  What happens: The dialog closes. Alert with role="alert": "Your subscription
  was

  changed somewhere else, so we didn't skip. We've refreshed it. Review the
  details and skip again if you still want to." The card reloads the data (with
  the skeleton over the old data) and never repeats the skip on its own. If the
  reload fails, the skip is disabled and "Try again" appears to reload
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Error: 500 or network
  What happens: The dialog stays open, with the error inside it ("Something
  went wrong. Your shipment was not skipped.") and a Try again button, which is
  an explicit click
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Error: 400
  What happens: Same treatment as the 409: the status changed, so I reload and
  the screen corrects itself (if it became paused, the skip disappears)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Error: 404
  What happens: Message "We couldn't find this subscription" with a reload
  button

  If the 409 happens twice in a row, the text starts saying "This is still
  changing" instead of repeating the same notice.

  5. Mobile first (375px)

  - Layout: a single column with a 16px side margin. All touch targets are at
    least 48px tall. The dialog buttons are stacked and full width.
  - Larger screens (≥600px): the card is centered with a maximum width of 600px.
    The "Next charge" block and the summary start sitting side by side, and the
    dialog buttons sit on the same line. It is simpler than Part B, on purpose.
  - Visual: reuses Part B's tokens (colors, radii 24/14/12, Inter and Fraunces).

  6. Accessibility

  - Dialog: native <dialog> with showModal(), which already gives focus locking
    and an inert background. Esc closes, except during sending. Focus returns
    to the skip button when closing.
  - Notices: the status container stays always mounted, with aria-live="polite"
    for success. Errors use role="alert".
  - Visible focus with the same green outline as Part B, and respect for
    prefers-reduced-motion.
  - Contrast: I will check the greenish gray #6B7A70, which is borderline on
    small text. Only secondary texts go in it, and the body uses
    --color-text-body.

  7. Structure, state and order

  Files:
  - SubscriptionCard.tsx and SubscriptionCard.module.css.
  - subscriptionReducer.ts: the reducer, so it can be tested on its own.
  - useSubscription.ts: the hook that connects reducer and API.
  - format.ts: currency, dates and nextChargeAfterSkip.
  - ItemList.tsx, SkipDialog.tsx and Notice.tsx.

  State (reducer):
  - load: loading | error | ready.
  - data.
  - skip: idle | confirming | submitting | submitError.
  - refreshing.
  - notice.

  Order:
  1. New tokens and moving formatPrice.
  2. Formatting, reducer and hook, with typecheck.
  3. Static UI per status (mobile first).
  4. Dialog and skip flow.
  5. Error paths and notices.
  6. Accessibility pass.
  7. Verification.

  8. How to test

  URLs, all with /?part=a:

  ┌───────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────┐
  │     Case      │               URL               │    What to check      │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │               │                                 │ Skeleton, then the    │
  │ Active        │ &fixture=active                 │ card. Thumbnail and   │
  │               │                                 │ placeholder. Total    │
  │               │                                 │ $101.87               │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Paused        │ &fixture=paused                 │ Pill and date. Skip   │
  │               │                                 │ absent from the DOM   │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Cancelled     │ &fixture=cancelled              │ Same                  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │               │                                 │ Alert and "Try again",│
  │ Error         │ &fixture=error                  │  which goes back to   │
  │               │                                 │ the skeleton and      │
  │               │                                 │ errors again          │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Unknown       │ &fixture=xyz                    │ 404 message           │
  │ fixture       │                                 │                       │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │               │                                 │ Skip → 409 → notice   │
  │ Conflict      │ &fixture=active&conflict=always │ and reloaded data, and│
  │               │                                 │  the second skip gives│
  │               │                                 │  409 again            │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │               │                                 │ Date advances 30 days.│
  │ Success       │ &conflict=never                 │ A second skip uses the│
  │               │                                 │  new updatedAt        │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │               │                                 │ Double click on "Yes, │
  │ Double click  │ &conflict=never                 │  skip" advances only  │
  │               │                                 │ 30 days, not 60       │
  └───────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────┘

  I also test Esc, focus, keyboard and 375px.

  About how to verify: for Part B I could only capture screens, without
  clicking. For Part A that is not enough. I propose installing puppeteer-core
  in a temporary folder outside the project, using the Chrome that is already
  installed, to run those flows for real. That includes Part B's interactive
  states, which were left only in the code. None of this goes into the
  repository.

  Decisions I am making

  1. Interface text in English, as in Part B.
  2. Skip as a secondary button and confirmation with focus on the safe button.
  3. Dialog locked during sending.
  4. 409 and 400 reload the data; 404 only shows a message.
  5. Two new tokens (--color-danger and --color-danger-bg) in tokens.css,
     because the brand palette has no error red. Paused reuses the badge's
     amber, and cancelled reuses the existing grays.
  6. Move formatPrice to src/shared/. That changes one import line in Part B.
  7. Paused and cancelled show the products without prices.

  Doubts (with the default I adopt if you do not answer)

  1. Several skips in a row: the API allows skipping again right after. The
     default is to allow it, always with the confirmation.
  2. Support contact: there is none in the project. The default is not to invent
     a link or phone number and to give only actionable steps.
  3. Resuming a paused or reactivating a cancelled one: the API does not have
     those actions. The default is to only inform the state, without a button.
  4. Date wording: the text speaks of "charge", never "ship", because the API
     only knows the charge date.

  Can I start with step 1?

> the plan is good but I want to make some changes
  1 - use "shipment" in buttons and titles (as in the test requirements:

● Implementation plan: Part A, "Manage my subscription" (v2)

  This version incorporates your six changes. The list of what was left out to
  keep the interface simple is at the end.

  API facts that shape the plan

  - skipNextShipment: requires the loaded updatedAt. The mock returns 409 when it
    does not match. With &conflict=always every skip gives 409, even after
    reloading. Without that parameter, the 409 happens in ≈1 of 3 attempts.
  - Actually skipping: Date.parse(nextChargeAt) + intervalDays * 24h in
    milliseconds, returned as ISO.
  - Errors: getSubscription gives 500 on fixture=error and 404 with an unknown
    fixture. The skip gives 400 if the status is not active and 404 if the id
    does not exist.
  - Values: nextChargeTotalCents already comes with the discount. With the
    fixtures' items, the subtotal is $119.85 and the total, $101.87.
  - Image: the Multivitamin has imageUrl: null.

  Text rule: "shipment" and "charge"

  - "Shipment" goes in buttons, titles and in the first sentence of the notices,
    that is, in the actions and in what happened.
  - "Charge" goes only in sentences about when or how much will be charged,
    including "no charge" on paused and cancelled subscriptions.
  - Outside those two words, I use "subscription" and short sentences.

  Interface texts, all following the rule:

  ┌─────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Where      │                         Text                          │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Card title      │ "Manage my subscription"                              │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Skip button     │ "Skip next shipment"                                  │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Dialog title    │ "Skip your next shipment?"                            │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Dialog body     │ "Your $101.87 charge on Nov 6 will move to Dec 6." and│
  │                 │  "Your subscription stays active."                    │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Dialog buttons  │ "Yes, skip shipment" / "Keep my shipment". While      │
  │                 │ sending: "Skipping…"                                  │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Success         │ title "Shipment skipped" and "Your $101.87 charge is  │
  │                 │ now on Dec 6 (was Nov 6)."                            │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ title "Your subscription changed" and "It was updated │
  │ Conflict (409)  │ somewhere else, so we didn't skip your shipment.      │
  │                 │ We've refreshed the details below. Check them and     │
  │                 │ skip again if you still want to."                     │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 500 or network  │ "Something went wrong. Your shipment wasn't skipped." │
  │ error (in the   │  with a "Try again" button                            │
  │ dialog)         │                                                       │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 400             │ "This subscription can't skip a shipment right now.   │
  │                 │ We've refreshed the details below."                   │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 404 on skip     │ "We couldn't find this subscription." with a "Reload" │
  │                 │ button                                                │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Reload failure  │ "We couldn't refresh your subscription. Skipping is   │
  │ after 409 or 400│ unavailable until it loads." with a "Try again"       │
  │                 │ button                                                │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Load failure    │ "We couldn't load your subscription." (for 404: "We   │
  │                 │ couldn't find your subscription.") with a "Try        │
  │                 │ again" button                                         │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Active          │ label "Next charge", line "$101.87 on Fri, Nov 6" and │
  │                 │  "Every 30 days"                                      │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Paused          │ "Paused until Nov 30" and "No charges while paused."  │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Cancelled       │ "Cancelled on Oct 19" and "No further charges."       │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Summary         │ "Subtotal", "Subscribe & save 15%" (the percentage    │
  │                 │ comes from the data) and "Total"                      │
  └─────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  1. States and visual differentiation

  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │    State     │                      What appears                        │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │              │ Simple skeleton in the card's shape. Region with         │
  │ Loading      │ aria-busy and the text "Loading your subscription…" only │
  │              │  for screen readers                                      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Error on     │ Message with role="alert" and a "Try again" button       │
  │ load         │                                                          │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Active       │ Green "Active" pill with an icon, a "Next charge" block  │
  │              │  and the skip button                                     │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │              │ Amber "Paused" pill (Part B's badge colors), a block     │
  │ Paused       │  with the date and products without prices. No skip      │
  │              │ button in the DOM                                        │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Cancelled    │ Gray "Cancelled" pill, a block with the date and         │
  │              │ products without prices. No skip button in the DOM       │
  └──────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The difference never depends only on color: each state has its own text and
  icon.

  2. Content of the active subscription

  - Header: title in Fraunces and the pill.
  - Next charge: "$101.87 on Fri, Nov 6" and "Every 30 days".
  - Products: 56px thumbnail, name, "Qty 2" and the line price (before the
    discount). A missing image becomes a tinted square with the product's
    initial.
  - Summary: Subtotal, discount and Total. The discount is computed as subtotal −
    nextChargeTotalCents, so the sum always closes with the value the API
    charges.

  3. Skip flow

  1. Button: "Skip next shipment" in a secondary style (outline).
  2. Dialog: shows the change with the old and the new date. The initial focus
     goes to "Keep my shipment", the safe button.
  3. Date preview (change 2): the function nextChargeAfterSkip(nextChargeAt,
     intervalDays) does exactly what the mock does: it adds intervalDays × 24 ×
     60 × 60 × 1000 ms to Date.parse(nextChargeAt). It does not use setDate. The
     mock does not export the one-day constant, so I define it with the same
     value and a comment pointing to it. With that, the dialog's date and the
     one that comes back from the server are the same instant, and the local
     formatting is identical in both.
  4. In progress: the reducer only accepts "send" from "confirming", so a second
     click does nothing. The button uses aria-disabled instead of disabled, to
     keep focus, and shows "Skipping…". The dialog's two buttons stay inert
     during sending.
  5. Success: the dialog closes, the card switches to the returned data (with the
     new updatedAt) and the success notice appears with role="status".

  4. Skip errors

  ┌────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Error    │                       What happens                         │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 409        │ The dialog closes. Alert with role="alert". The card       │
  │            │ reloads the data. The skip is never repeated on its own    │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 500 or     │ The dialog stays open, with the error inside it and the    │
  │ network    │ "Try again" button, which is an explicit click             │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 400        │ Same treatment as the 409: the status changed, so I reload │
  │            │  and the screen corrects itself                            │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 404        │ Message and a reload button                                │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Reload     │ The skip is disabled, and "Try again" redoes only the      │
  │ fails      │ loading                                                    │
  └────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  5. Mobile first (375px)

  - Layout: a single column with a 16px side margin. All touch targets are at
    least 48px tall. The dialog buttons are stacked and full width.
  - Larger screens (≥600px): the card is centered with a maximum width of
    560px, and the dialog buttons sit on the same line. Nothing beyond that.

  6. Accessibility and contrast

  Dialog (change 4): native <dialog> with showModal(), opened and closed from the
  reducer's state.
  - Esc during sending: the cancel event handler calls preventDefault() when the
    skip is in "submitting". Outside sending, Esc closes normally and close
    syncs the state to "idle".
  - Click on the backdrop during sending: the click handler on the <dialog>
    itself (target equal to the element, that is, the backdrop) is ignored while
    in "submitting". Outside sending, it closes the dialog.
  - Browser limitation: Chrome may close the dialog on a second consecutive Esc,
    even with preventDefault(). To cover that, the close handler reopens the
    dialog with showModal() if the state is still "submitting".
  - Focus: on closing, focus returns to the skip button.

  Contrast (change 3): I measured #6B7A70 with the WCAG formula:

  ┌──────────────────────┬─────────────────┬────────────────┐
  │      Background      │ #6B7A70 (current)│ #627067 (new)  │
  ├──────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Cream #FBF8F3        │ 4.27            │ 4.92           │
  ├──────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Pale green #F3F6F2   │ 4.15            │ 4.78           │
  ├──────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ White                │ 4.52            │ 5.21           │
  └──────────────────────┴─────────────────┴────────────────┘

  The new hex for the card's secondary texts is #627067, which passes AA (4.5:1)
  on all of the card's backgrounds. I do not change the existing
  --color-text-muted, because Part B has to stay identical to the reference. I
  create a new token, --color-text-secondary: #627067, used only in Part A. The
  body text stays on --color-text-body (#4A5550, between 7.1 and 7.8).

  Notices: the status container stays always mounted, with aria-live="polite"
  for success. Errors use role="alert". The visible focus uses the same green
  outline as Part B.

  7. Structure, state and order

  Files:
  - SubscriptionCard.tsx and SubscriptionCard.module.css.
  - subscriptionReducer.ts: the reducer, testable on its own.
  - useSubscription.ts: the hook that connects reducer and API.
  - format.ts: currency, dates and nextChargeAfterSkip.
  - ItemList.tsx, SkipDialog.tsx and Notice.tsx.
  - In tokens.css: --color-text-secondary: #627067, --color-danger: #A32A20 and
    --color-danger-bg: #FCEDEB (the red has 6.3 to 7.2:1 on the card's
    backgrounds).

  State (reducer): load (loading | error | ready), data, skip (idle |
  confirming | submitting | submitError), refreshing and notice.

  Loading race (change 5):
  - The hook keeps a request counter. Each load call increments the counter and
    captures its id. The response is only applied if the id is still the most
    recent and the component is still mounted.
  - The effect's cleanup also increments the counter. Under StrictMode, the
    first (simulated) run of the effect is discarded and the second one counts.
    The mock receives two calls in development, which is harmless.
  - The same mechanism covers "Try again" and the reload after a 409, so that
    overlapping calls never let an old response overwrite a new one.
  - The skip is single-flight through the reducer, and a response that arrives
    after unmounting is ignored.

  Order:
  1. New tokens and moving formatPrice to src/shared/.
  2. Formatting, reducer and hook, with typecheck.
  3. Static UI per status (mobile first).
  4. Dialog and skip flow.
  5. Error paths and notices.
  6. Accessibility pass.
  7. Verification.

  8. How to test

  URLs, all with /?part=a:

  Case: Active
  URL: &fixture=active
  What to check: Skeleton, then the card. Multivitamin placeholder. Total
  $101.87
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Paused
  URL: &fixture=paused
  What to check: Pill and date. Skip absent from the DOM
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Cancelled
  URL: &fixture=cancelled
  What to check: Same
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Error
  URL: &fixture=error
  What to check: Alert and "Try again", which goes back to the skeleton and
  errors again
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Unknown fixture
  URL: &fixture=xyz
  What to check: 404 message
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Conflict
  URL: &fixture=active&conflict=always
  What to check: Skip → 409 → notice and reloaded data, and the second skip gives
  409 again
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Success
  URL: &conflict=never
  What to check: The date changes to exactly the one shown in the dialog, and a
  second
  skip uses the new updatedAt
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Double click
  URL: &conflict=never
  What to check: Double click on "Yes, skip shipment" advances only 30 days, not
  60
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: Esc and backdrop
  URL: any that reaches sending
  What to check: Esc and a click on the backdrop do not close during "Skipping…"
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Case: StrictMode and Try again
  URL: &fixture=error
  What to check: Repeated clicks on "Try again" do not let an old response
  overwrite the new one

  How to verify: I install puppeteer-core in a temporary folder outside the
  project, using the Chrome that is already installed, to run those flows for
  real, including 375px. That also covers Part B's interactive states that were
  only checked through the code. None of this goes into the repository.

  Decisions I keep making

  1. Text in English, as in Part B.
  2. Skip as a secondary button, with initial focus on the safe button.
  3. 409 and 400 reload the data; 404 only shows a message.
  4. Paused and cancelled show the products without prices.
  5. Move formatPrice to src/shared/, which changes one import line in Part B.
  6. Confirmation button: "Yes, skip shipment". It is my choice, because the
     requirements only give the other three texts.

  Doubts (with the default I adopt if you do not answer)

  1. Several skips in a row: the API allows skipping again right after. The
     default is to allow it, always with the confirmation.
  2. Support contact: there is none in the project. The default is not to invent
     a link or phone number.
  3. Resuming a paused or reactivating a cancelled one: the API does not have
     those actions. The default is to only inform the state, without a button.

  Out of scope (change 6)

  To keep the interface simple, the following were left out: animations (and the
  reduced-motion check), the "in N days", grayscale thumbnails, a two-column
  layout, help text under the button and message variations for repeated
  conflicts. The effort goes into the correct behavior of the states and the
  errors.

  Read 2 files (ctrl+o to expand)
● Part A is ready and tested in the browser at 375px: 109 checks passed across
  the scenarios in the table and the extras I added. In the first round it was
  105 passing and 2 failing, and the two failures are explained below. The
  typecheck passed at each step and everything is committed and pushed, without
  co-authorship.

  What passed

  - Active, paused, cancelled and error:
    - Active: shows the skeleton while loading, the total $101.87, the summary
      $119.85 − $17.98, the placeholder for the missing image and buttons of at
      least 44px.
    - Paused and cancelled: the skip button does not even exist in the DOM.
    - Error: shows the alert, and "Try again" works, including with repeated
      clicks.
    - Unknown fixture: shows the 404 message.
  - Dialog:
    - The initial focus goes to "Keep my shipment".
    - The date shown is the old one and the new one (+30 days).
    - Tab never reaches the page behind it.
    - Esc and a click on the backdrop close it when idle.
    - Focus returns to the skip button.
  - While sending:
    - Esc (twice), a click on the backdrop and "Keep my shipment" do not close
      the dialog.
    - A double click generates a single API call, and the date advances only 30
      days, not 60.
  - Success: the date predicted in the dialog is identical to the server's. The
    notice says "(was …)", and the second skip goes through using the new
    updatedAt.
  - &conflict=always: the 409 is explained, the data is reloaded and the skip is
    never repeated on its own. A second attempt by the customer also works.
  - Random mode: both outcomes appeared, and the app remained usable.
  - Failures injected in the browser:
    - 500 on skip: the error appears inside the dialog and "Try again" works.
    - 400 and 404 on skip.
    - Reload failure after the 409: the skip is disabled and the app recovers
      later.
  - Out-of-order responses: the old, slow response does not overwrite the new
    one.
  - Layout: no horizontal scroll in any state. At 1200px the card is limited to
    560px and centered.
  - Part B: I really tested what I had previously only checked through the code
    (option change, 60 days, quantity 2 and the limit of 99).

  What failed, and what I did

  1. Focus after a 409: it was a real bug. The skip button was disabled during
     the reload, and focus fell to the page. I changed to aria-disabled, as was
     already the case in the dialog. Fixed and covered by a test.
  2. Tab and the dialog: it was a test limitation, not the app's. The native
     dialog lets Tab leave for the browser interface. I changed the criterion
     to "focus never reaches the page behind" and it passed. I changed the
     test, so it is worth you knowing.

  I also found a text gap while building the scenarios: if the 409 and the
  reload failed together, the notice did not say that the skip was not done. I
  adjusted the text.

  Less certain

  - Repeated Esc: it passed in Chrome, but I do not know whether the branch that
    reopens the dialog was exercised or whether the dialog simply did not close.
    Firefox and Safari were not tested.
  - Screen readers: I only checked the structure (live regions mounted, labeled
    dialog). I did not listen with NVDA or VoiceOver.
  - 375px: it is viewport emulation in Chrome, not a real phone. Touch, virtual
    keyboard and real scrolling went untested.
  - Injected failures: 500, 400, 404 and the reload failure were only exercised
    with a tampered mock in the browser. The real API may behave differently.
  - Dates: the preview and the server use the same instant, but I only tested in
    my time zone. The tests compute the expected date with the current time, so
    they may fail near midnight.
  - Reload that changes the status: I could not produce, with the mock, a reload
    that returns "paused". That path, in which the button disappears, is
    covered only by the logic, not by a test.
  - Contrast: I calculated the ratios of the secondary text and of the red (4.78:1
    and 6.34:1 in the worst cases), but I did not run an automated audit on the
    rendered pages.
  - Visual: there was no design to compare against. What is good or not is my
    own judgment, only confirmed on the 375px captures.

  Commits

  The Part A commits are in the Raicom-test repository, on the main branch:
  tokens and formatPrice moved to src/shared/, logic (reducer and hook),
  interface and the focus fix.

  Outside the repository

  The tests stayed in the session's temporary folder, because the project has
  no test runner. If you want, I can add them to the repository with a
  development dependency. The development server (port 5199) is still running
  in the background.

> Let's talk about layout. Afterwards I will do the necessary tests. This screen
  ended up looking
  like a shopping cart, and subscription and next shipment are different things.
  I want two columns:

  - Current subscription column: the subscription's items (with quantity and
  image), the frequency,
    the discount and the status. Here go the actions of the subscription as a
  whole: skipping shipments and
    "Cancel subscription".
  - Next shipment column: the next shipment's items, the date and the values in
  detail.

  Two actions are demonstrative: the API does not have them, but they make sense
  because the customer
  should not have to come back every month to skip the next shipment. Neither of
  the two calls the API
  nor changes state:
  1. "Cancel subscription": when clicked, it opens a dialog, with customer-facing
  and
     honest text, for example "Cancellation isn't available yet", with a button
  to close.
  2. Skipping several shipments: the customer can choose how many shipments to
  skip (for example, 1 to 3).
     With 1, the real skip that already exists applies, with the confirmation
  and the API. With more
     than 1, it opens a dialog informing that skipping several shipments is not
  available yet and that today you can only
     skip one at a time. Propose how the control looks on screen so the
  customer understands that
     the feature is limited, without looking broken.
     The explanation that these two actions are improvement suggestions for the
  API does not go in the
     interface: it goes to the project's README (API improvement suggestions
  section).

  What do you think of what I suggested? Would you add anything else? If you
  disagree with something,
  say so and propose an alternative.

  Think like a senior user experience (UX) designer, with good judgment, and
  stay within the
  scope of the project, taking care of a subscription management flow.
  Prioritize: clarity of
  what comes next (date and amount), visual hierarchy between information and
  action, error
  prevention on the skip and messages that say what happened and what to do. For
  each important
  layout decision, explain in one sentence why.

  Context that should weigh on your opinion:
  - The API only allows skipping one shipment per call. There is no cancel,
  pause or resume.
  - Mobile first (375px): say how the two columns are stacked and which comes
  first.
    Two columns from ~768px, with a wider card on desktop.
  - Subscriptions with status paused or cancelled continue without skip actions
  and with a
    different look from the active one. Say what the next shipment column shows
  in those cases and which of the

● I agree with two columns and with treating the two actions as demonstrative. I
  disagree on one point, the place of the skip, and I add two observations. Still
  no code.

  What I think of what you suggested

  1. Two columns: I agree.
  - Subscription (what you have) and next shipment (what is going to happen) are
    different concepts.
  - Today the screen mixes the two in a single block, and that is what creates
    the shopping-cart look.

  2. The items appear in both columns, and that needs care.
  - The API only has one list of items. The "next shipment" has exactly the same
    products as the subscription.
  - Showing the same list twice, identical, looks like a bug.
  - Proposal: the subscription shows the items with image and quantity (what you
    subscribed to). The next shipment shows the same items in a compact,
    text-only list, without images and without a price per line (what goes in
    the package).
  - The two lists would only diverge if the API one day allows changing a
    specific shipment. That goes in the README.

  3. Where the skip goes: I disagree.
  - You put the skip in the subscription column. My recommendation is the next
    shipment column.
  - Why: the action changes the date and the amount that are there. The customer
    sees what is changing next to the button, and the confirmation dialog does
    not surprise them.
  - In the subscription column go the status, the facts (frequency, discount),
    the items and "Cancel subscription".
  - The quantity selector sits with the skip, because 1 is the real skip.
  - If you prefer your original arrangement, I will implement it. In that case I
    would repeat the next shipment's date near the control.

  4. The order of the columns changes.
  - I recommend the next shipment first, both on mobile (on top) and on desktop
    (on the left). The customer's main question is "what is coming and when".
  - The DOM order stays equal to the visual one, without CSS order, so that
    keyboard focus follows the reading.
  - It is a trivial swap if you prefer the subscription first.

  What I would add:
  - Previewing the effect of the skip: discreet, without creating a new action.
    We already have that in the confirmation dialog, so I do not touch it.
  - Cancel is a dead end: the text has to be honest about what happens
    (nothing) and what remains valid. Depending on what you answer below, I can
    give a real next step.

  Layout decisions (each with the why)

  ┌───────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐

  Desktop (≥768px), page with a maximum width of ~880px, two equal columns and

  Mobile (375px):
  - Stacked, next shipment first: the date and the amount appear right on the
    first screen, and the skip button is one scroll away.
  - Then: the subscription panel, with the status at the top, the items, the
    facts and "Cancel subscription" at the end.
  - The notices stay full width under the title, so they appear in any order.
  - Between 600 and 767px: a single centered column, with a maximum of 560px.

  Control for skipping several shipments

  - Form: three options "1 / 2 / 3" as segments (native radios inside a
    fieldset, with arrow-key navigation), each with a minimum height of 48px.
  - Always-visible notice: "For now, shipments can only be skipped one at a
    time." This gives context before the click and does not leave the limitation
    as a surprise.
  - 2 and 3 are in a lighter gray, but remain selectable.
  - The button label follows: "Skip next shipment" with 1, and "Skip next 2
    shipments" with 2.
  - Clicking with 2 or 3 opens an informational dialog:
    - Title: "Skipping several shipments isn't available yet".
    - Body: "For now you can skip one shipment at a time. Once your next
      shipment is skipped, you can skip the one after it."
    - One button, "Close". On closing, the selector goes back to 1, so the
      customer returns to the path that works. That is only the screen's local
      state, not the subscription's.
  - Why not "Soon" on the options: it would promise a date that I do not have.

  "Cancel subscription"

  - Dialog: title "Cancellation isn't available yet". Body: "We can't cancel a
    subscription from this page yet. Nothing was changed: your subscription is
    active and your next charge is still on Wed, Nov 4." One button, "Close".
  - If the subscription is paused, the second sentence becomes just "Your
    subscription hasn't changed."

  Paused and cancelled

  Paused
  Next shipment column: Amber block "No shipment scheduled" with "Paused until

  Nov 22. No charges while paused." No items, no amounts, no skip
  Subscription column: "Paused" pill, items, frequency and discount. "Cancel
  subscription" appears (a customer may want to cancel a paused subscription)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Cancelled
  Next shipment column: Gray block "No shipment scheduled" with "Cancelled on

  Oct 19. No further charges."
  Subscription column: "Cancelled" pill and items. No action. I hide frequency
  and discount, because they would be misleading

  The several-shipments selector and the skip never appear outside the active
  state, and the next shipment panel stays present in both cases so the layout
  does not jump.

  What changes in the structure

  - SubscriptionCard.tsx becomes the container: title, notices, two-column grid
    and the dialogs.
  - New: NextShipmentPanel, SubscriptionPanel, SkipControl (selector, notice and
    button), ChargeDetails (replaces Summary), ShipmentItems (the compact list)
    and InfoDialog (used by the two demonstrative dialogs).
  - Refactoring: I extract from SkipDialog the native <dialog> base (open, close,
    Esc, backdrop). SkipDialog and InfoDialog reuse it.
  - Local screen state: useState for the chosen quantity and for which
    informational dialog is open. The API's reducer and hook do not change.
  - Kept unchanged: the real skip flow, the 409 and the other errors, the
    notices, the live regions and the tokens.
  - Tests: I need to update the existing scenarios (the texts and the position
    of some elements change) and add the selector, the two dialogs and the
    columns of the paused and cancelled subscriptions.

● Updated plan: two-column layout (Part A)

  I incorporated your answers. Two of them call for a longer explanation, in
  points 2 and 3 below. Still no code.

  What changed relative to my previous proposal

  1. Skip in the next shipment column: kept, as per my suggestion.
  2. Order on desktop: yours applies. The subscription stays on the left and the
     next shipment on the right, read from left to right. On mobile the next
     shipment comes first.
  3. Price per line: it stays in the next shipment panel and leaves the
     subscription panel.
  4. Cancel on paused and cancelled: it appears on paused and does not appear on
     cancelled.
  5. No contact channel: the texts keep saying only what did not change.
  6. README: receives all the suggestions.

  1. Different order on desktop and on mobile

  Desktop reads subscription → next shipment, and mobile reads next shipment →
  subscription. This creates an accessibility problem that I want to make
  explicit.

  - If I use CSS order: the keyboard and the screen reader would follow the HTML
    order, while the eye follows the visual order. Focus would jump from one side
    to the other at one of the two widths.
  - My solution: a small useMediaQuery("(min-width: 768px)") hook decides the
    order in which the two panels are rendered. The HTML stays equal to the
    visual order at both widths, and without order.
  - Cost: if the window crosses 768px with an active focus, focus may be lost.
    It is a rare case, and a real customer's window almost never changes like
    that.
  - Between 600 and 767px: a single column, with the mobile order.

  With the next shipment on the right, the skip action sits at the end of the
  reading path: the customer sees what they subscribed to, then what is coming,
  and then the action. So that the eye goes to the next shipment even though it
  is second, that panel has a tinted background and a large date. The
  subscription stays white, calmer.

  2. Layout

  Desktop (≥768px), maximum width of ~880px, two equal columns and 24px between
  them:
  Manage my subscription
  [ notice, full width, only when one exists ]
  ┌ YOUR SUBSCRIPTION [✓ Active] ┐ ┌ NEXT SHIPMENT  (tinted background) ┐
  │ [img] Vitamin D, 60 softgels │ │ Wed, Nov 4                     │
  │       Qty 1                  │ │ $101.87 will be charged        │
  │ [ M ] Multivitamin, 30 tabl. │ │                                │
  │       Qty 2                  │ │ Vitamin D, 60 softgels ×1 $29.95│
  │                              │ │ Multivitamin, 30 tablets ×2 $89.90│
  │ Delivery  Every 30 days      │ │ Subtotal              $119.85  │
  │ Discount  Subscribe & save 15%│ │ Subscribe & save 15%  −$17.98  │
  │                              │ │ Total                 $101.87  │
  │ ──────────────────────────── │ │ ────────────────────────────── │
  │ Cancel subscription          │ │ Shipments to skip              │
  └──────────────────────────────┘ │ ( 1 ) ( 2 ) ( 3 )              │

  Mobile (375px): the title, the notices at full width, the next shipment (date,
  amount, items, details and skip) and lastly the subscription (status, items,
  facts and "Cancel subscription"). The two panels have a 16px side margin and
  16px between them.

  4. Paused and cancelled

  Paused
  Next shipment: Amber block "No shipment scheduled" with "Paused until Nov 22.
  No
  charges while paused." No items, no amounts, no skip
  Subscription: "Paused" pill, items, frequency and discount. "Cancel
  subscription" appears
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Cancelled
  Next shipment: Gray block "No shipment scheduled" with "Cancelled on Oct 19.
  No
  further charges."
  Subscription: "Cancelled" pill and items. No frequency and discount, no action

  The selector and the skip never appear outside the active state.

  5. Control for several shipments

  - Three options "1 / 2 / 3" as segments (native radios in a fieldset, with
    keyboard arrows), each with a minimum height of 48px. Options 2 and 3 are
    lighter, but remain selectable.
  - Always-visible notice: "For now, shipments can only be skipped one at a
    time."
  - The button follows the choice: "Skip next shipment" with 1 and "Skip next 2
    shipments" with 2 or more.
  - With 1, it is the real skip that already exists, with confirmation and API.
  - With 2 or more, it opens an informational dialog, without calling the API:
    "Skipping several shipments isn't available yet" / "For now you can skip one
    shipment at a time. Once your next shipment is skipped, you can skip the one
    after it." One button, "Close". On closing, the selector goes back to 1
    (local state, without touching the subscription).

  6. "Cancel subscription"

  Dialog: "Cancellation isn't available yet" / "We can't cancel a subscription
  from this page yet. Nothing was changed: your subscription is active and your
  next charge is still on Wed, Nov 4." One button, "Close". If the subscription
  is paused, the second sentence is just "Your subscription hasn't changed."

  7. What changes in the structure

  - SubscriptionCard.tsx: becomes the container (title, notices, grid and
    dialogs) and uses useMediaQuery to decide the order of the panels.
  - New: SubscriptionPanel, NextShipmentPanel, SkipControl, ChargeDetails
    (replaces Summary), ShipmentItems (with price per line), InfoDialog and
    useMediaQuery.
  - Changed: ItemList loses the price and keeps only image and quantity.
    SkipDialog starts using a shared native <dialog> base, extracted into a
    ModalDialog (open, close, Esc, backdrop), which InfoDialog also uses.
  - Local state: useState for the chosen quantity and for which informational
    dialog is open. The API's reducer and hook do not change.
  - Kept: the real skip flow, the 409 and the other errors, the notices, the live
    regions, the "shipment/charge" rule and the tokens. No new token.

  8. README

  "API improvement suggestions" section, with none of this in the interface:
  1. Cancellation: an endpoint to cancel, with an effective date.
  2. Skipping several shipments: a quantity parameter in the call, keeping
     expectedUpdatedAt.
  3. Resuming a paused subscription and reactivating a cancelled one.
  4. Items per shipment: the API has only one list of items, so the two panels
     show the same products. A nextShipment.items would let them diverge.

  9. Build order

  Typecheck at each step and one commit with push per step, without
  co-authorship.
  1. Extract the ModalDialog from SkipDialog, without changing the behavior, and
     rerun the skip tests to catch regressions.
  2. useMediaQuery, the two panels, ShipmentItems, ChargeDetails, the ItemList
     without price and the grid CSS, all with static content per status.
  3. SkipControl, with the real skip button moved inside it and the selector
     with the notice.
  4. InfoDialog and the two demonstrative dialogs, with the selector going back
     to 1 on closing.
  5. README.
  6. Update and expand the tests and run the full battery.

  10. Tests

  - Order at 375px and 1200px: at 1200px the subscription is to the left of the
    next shipment, and at 375px the next shipment comes first. Tab follows the
    visual order in both cases.
  - Width: no horizontal scroll at 375, 768 and 1200px, and a single column
    between 600 and 767px.
  - Selector: starts at 1, the keyboard arrows work, the button label follows,
    and on closing the dialog it goes back to 1.
  - Several shipments: opens the informational dialog without calling the API
    (zero skip calls).
  - Cancel: opens the dialog without any call and without changing the date. It
    appears on paused and does not appear on cancelled.
  - Prices: the line with a price appears in the next shipment and not in the
    subscription.
  - Regression: the skip flow, the errors, the focus, the double click and the
    out-of-order responses keep passing, with the selectors updated.

  Decisions I made on my own

  - Swapping the order in the HTML through useMediaQuery, instead of CSS order.
  - The maximum width of ~880px and the 768px breakpoint, because they are the
    ones you indicated.
  - The tinted background on the next shipment panel to compensate for its
    position in the reading.

  Can I start with step 1?

      11  - **Part B:** `src/part-b/BuyBox.tsx`. Open `/?part=b`. Match `
