      13  You can add any libraries you like.
      14 +                                                               
      15 +## Part A: how the screen is organised                         
      16 +                                                               
      17 +Two panels: **Your subscription** (status, products with image 
         +and quantity, delivery frequency, discount) and **Next shipment
         +** (date and amount first, then the lines, the charge details a
         +nd the skip). From 768px they sit side by side, the subscriptio
         +n on the left and the next shipment on the right. On a phone th
         +e next shipment comes first. The order is decided in the DOM wi
         +th a synchronous `useMediaQuery`, not with CSS `order`, so keyb
         +oard and screen reader order match what is on screen.          
      18 +                                                               
      19 +Paused and cancelled subscriptions never offer a skip. The next
         + shipment panel says why there is none (paused until, or cancel
         +led on). Skipping a shipment always asks for confirmation, show
         +s the old and the new charge date, and is never retried automat
         +ically after a rejection.                                      
      20 +                                                               
      21 +Two actions on the screen are placeholders. They do not call th
         +e API and do not change anything; they open a dialog saying the
         + feature isn't available yet:                                  
      22 +                                                               
      23 +- **Cancel subscription** (active and paused subscriptions).   
      24 +- **Skipping more than one shipment** (the 1 / 2 / 3 selector; 
         +1 is the real skip).                                           
      25 +                                                               
      26 +They exist because customers should not have to come back every
         + month to skip the next shipment, and because cancelling is som
         +ething people look for on this screen. The API has no endpoint 
         +for either, hence the suggestions below.                       
      27 +                                                               
      28 +## API improvement suggestions                                 
      29 +                                                               
      30 +1. **Cancel a subscription.** An endpoint that cancels and retu
         +rns the updated subscription with the date it takes effect, usi
         +ng `expectedUpdatedAt` like the skip does so a stale screen can
         +not cancel by accident.                                        
      31 +2. **Skip several shipments in one call.** A `count` (or a "ski
         +p until" date) on the skip call, keeping `expectedUpdatedAt`, a
         +nd returning the new `nextChargeAt`. Today the screen can only 
         +skip one shipment at a time.                                   
      32 +3. **Resume a paused subscription and reactivate a cancelled on
         +e.** The data model has `pausedUntil` and `cancelledAt`, but no
         + way to act on them, so a paused or cancelled customer cannot d
         +o anything from this screen.                                   
      33 +4. **Items per shipment.** The API returns one list of items. T
         +he next shipment panel and the subscription panel therefore sho
         +w the same products. A `nextShipment.items` (with its own quant
         +ities and prices) would let them differ, for example for a one-
         +off add-on.                                                    
      34 +5. **Error bodies.** Rejections only carry a status and a short
         + message. A stable error `code` (for example `subscription_chan
         +ged`, `not_active`) would let the screen choose its wording wit
         +hout relying on the HTTP status alone.

● Bash(cd "C:/Nimue-Digital/Railcom/natura-wellness-frontend-test/natura-wellnes

  Read 3 files (ctrl+o to expand)

● I implemented the six steps of the plan and the test battery is green. It is 204
  checks passing and 0 failing, in two full runs in a row. The typecheck passed
  at every step, and each step became a commit with a push, without
  co-authorship. The development server (port 5199) is still running.

  What was done

  1. Shared dialog base (ModalDialog), without changing behavior.
  2. Two-panel layout, with the order decided in the DOM by a synchronous
     useMediaQuery. The skeleton follows the two panels in the order of each
     width.
  3. 1/2/3 selector with the fixed notice and the informational dialog.
  4. "Cancel subscription", which appears on active and paused and disappears on
     cancelled.
  5. README with the section of suggestions for the API.
  6. Tests.

  Deviation from the plan: the InfoDialog came in at step 3, and not 4, because
  the selector already needed it.

  What passed

  - Order of the panels:
    - At 375px the next shipment comes first, and at 1200px the subscription is
      on the left.
    - At 767px the mobile order applies and at 768px the desktop one.
    - The order never changes from the first render to the loaded page, in the
      three fixtures and at both widths.
    - The Tab order follows the visual order.
  - Content:
    - The next shipment shows the date, the amount and the price per line. The
      subscription does not show any price.
    - Paused and cancelled show the reason in the next shipment column, without
      any skip action.
    - Cancel appears only on paused.
  - Selector:
    - It starts at 1 and works with the keyboard arrows. The button label follows
      the choice.
    - With 2 or 3, the dialog opens without any API call. On closing, the
      selector goes back to 1.
  - Cancel: the dialog calls nothing nor changes anything, and the text does not
    invent a contact.
  - Regression: the real skip, the 409, the 400 and the 404, the 500 inside the
    dialog, the reload failure, the double click, the out-of-order responses and
    the focus keep passing.
  - Layout: no horizontal scroll at 375, 600, 700, 768 and 1200px, in all
    states.
  - Part B: the interactions keep working.

  What failed, and what it was

  In the first round 3 checks failed. After one fix others appeared, and all of
  them were resolved:
  - A real bug, which I fixed in the app: a late or paired close event from the
    browser undid what the user chose right afterward. The selector went back to
    1 and the next click opened the real skip.
    - My first fix (a flag) was wrong: it only "remembered" one pending event.
    - The final fix is simpler: Esc is handled on the cancel event, and close
      only matters if the browser closed the dialog by itself.
    - The scenario that used to fail in 2 of 3 runs passes 5 out of 5.
  - Problems with the test script:
    - A selector picked the "Close" button of a closed dialog.
    - A read ran ahead of the app.
    - A JavaScript .click() does not generate user activation, so Chrome closes
      on Esc without firing cancel.

  Less certain

  - I only tested in Chrome. The handling of Esc and of the second Esc while the
    send runs was not verified in Firefox or Safari.
  - Dialog events: the delay and pairing of the close events were observed in
    Chrome without user activation. A real click may behave differently. That is
    why the fix does not depend on counting events.
  - Resizing with an active focus: I only confirmed that the order flips and that
    the content survives. I did not test what happens to focus, nor with an open
    dialog.
  - Screen readers: I only checked the structure (group with legend, live
    regions, labeled dialogs). I did not listen with NVDA or VoiceOver.
  - 375px is viewport emulation, not a real phone.
  - Injected failures (500, 400, 404, reload failure, out-of-order responses) use
    a tampered mock only in the browser. The real API may behave differently.
  - Dates: the tests compute the expected date with the current time, so they may
    fail near midnight. I only tested in my time zone.
  - Tab: I checked the first three stops, not the whole page.
  - Part B: I moved formatPrice and the interactions pass, but I did not rerun
    the pixel comparison with the reference. I did not touch any of its styles.
  - Visual: the selector's notice wraps onto two lines at 375px and leaves
    "time." alone on the second. On paused, the next shipment panel is tall and
    almost empty because the columns have the same height. Both are design
    judgments, with no reference to compare against.
  - The tests are not in the repository. They run from a temporary folder,
    because the project has no test runner. I can add them, with a development
    dependency.

  Commits

  de4c940, faead3f, 9c7f3d7, f2b555c, 6e595df and d735935, all on the main
  branch.

  Do you want me to fix the wrapping of the selector's notice, or do you prefer
  to move on to something else?

> 1. Replace the 1/2/3 selector with a native <select> to choose how many
  shipments to skip (1 to 6).
     Each option shows the result, for example "2 shipments · next charge Jan
  3" (calculated
     with the same calculation as the skip, adding intervalDays per shipment).
  Keep the notice "For now,
     shipments can only be skipped one at a time" visible. The button keeps the
  label that
     follows the choice. With 1, it is the real skip. With 2 or more, the click
  on the button opens the

  block (section with

● Below I describe what will change and the trade-offs that the second change
  brings. I have not altered anything yet.

  1. Native <select> from 1 to 6 shipments

  How it looks
  - A "Shipments to skip" label linked to a native <select> (the fieldset with
    radios goes away). The select is 48px tall, takes the width of the panel and
    uses the same chevron style as Part B, with the tokens.
  - The notice "For now, shipments can only be skipped one at a time." stays
    visible and describes the select and the button (aria-describedby).
  - The button keeps the label that follows the choice: "Skip next shipment" with
    1 and "Skip next N shipments" with 2 or more.
  - Changing the select never opens a dialog. The informational dialog only opens
    when clicking the button with 2 or more, without calling the API. With 1, the
    click opens the real skip's confirmation, as today.
  - When closing the informational dialog, the choice goes back to 1, as it
    already works today. If you prefer to keep the choice, it is one line.

  The options. Each option is "N shipment(s) · next charge DATE". The text
  separates "shipment" (the action) from "charge" (date and amount), per the
  rule. With the fixtures' data, the base date is Wed, Nov 4:

  ┌────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Option │                 Text                  │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 1      │ 1 shipment · next charge Dec 4        │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2      │ 2 shipments · next charge Jan 3, 2027 │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 3      │ 3 shipments · next charge Feb 2, 2027 │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 4      │ 4 shipments · next charge Mar 4, 2027 │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 5      │ 5 shipments · next charge Apr 3, 2027 │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 6      │ 6 shipments · next charge May 3, 2027 │
  └────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

  The calculation. nextChargeAt + N × intervalDays × 24h, in milliseconds, with
  the same arithmetic as the skip. Since it is a sum of integers, the result is
  identical to that of N skips in a row on the server. I reuse the
  nextChargeAfterSkip function generalized for N (with N = 1 the value is the
  same as today, so the confirmation dialog does not change).

  Two details for you to decide
  - The year: your sample shows "Jan 3" with no year. My current rule puts the
    year when it differs from the current year. With 6 shipments the date
    crosses the year, so "Jan 3, 2027" avoids ambiguity. I propose keeping the
    year and only omitting it when it is the current year.
  - Daylight saving time: since the calculation is exact in milliseconds (like
    the server), passing through a clock change shifts the local time by 1h. The
    date only changes if the time is near midnight. It is consistent with the
    server, but it may surprise. I am informing you so it is not a surprise.

  2. A single block, with the block's width driving the layout

  Structure. A <section aria-labelledby> inside the existing <main>, containing
  the title, the notices and the two panels. The dialogs stay outside it, in the
  <main>.

  Light look. The block itself has no border, background, shadow or radius: it is
  just semantic grouping and spacing. That way there is no card inside a card,
  and the panels are the only cards. To make them lighter, I propose reducing the
  panels' radius from 24px to 14px and making the subscription panel
  transparent, with only a thin border. The next shipment panel keeps the
  pale-green background.

  Your sentence ended in ", ." If something was missing, tell me.

  Title level. I keep h1, because this demo page has no other h1. If the block is
  embedded in a larger page, it is one line to become h2.

  Container queries (columns)
  - The block becomes a container (container-type: inline-size). The two columns
    and the panels' padding start depending on its width with @container.
  - The proposed threshold is 720px. Before, the desktop started with the window
    at 768px, which gave a 736px block. With 720px the common case stays the
    same, and a narrow block inside a wide window (for example, in a sidebar)
    uses the phone order.

  Width observer (panel order). A useContainerWidth hook observes the block with
  ResizeObserver and decides the order in the DOM, as today. The useMediaQuery
  hook stops being used and I remove it.

  Trade-offs you asked me to explain beforehand

  1. The observer is asynchronous, so the first render does not know the width. A
     measurement by ResizeObserver arrives after the layout. To avoid a "jump", I
     do a synchronous measurement in a useLayoutEffect, before painting, and
     only render the panels (and the skeleton) after that measurement. The title
     and the notices appear first, with no order to get wrong. The DOM never goes
     through a wrong order, and the "the order never changes" test remains
     valid.
  2. A frame of mismatch when resizing. When the width changes, the CSS switches
     columns right away, but the order in the DOM depends on a React state. If
     that state only updated on the next frame, there would be a frame with the
     old order. To avoid it, the observer's callback commits the change
     synchronously (flushSync), before painting.
  3. The threshold lives in two places. The CSS cannot read a TypeScript
     constant, so the 720px exist in the @container and in the hook. I leave a
     comment in both and a test that forces the block to 719px and 720px and
     checks that columns and order change together. The alternative would be to
     drive the columns from the same boolean of the hook (a single source of
     truth), but you asked for container queries.
  4. The container creates layout containment. It becomes the reference block for
     positioned descendants, and its width cannot depend on the content. Nothing
     on the screen depends on that today. The <dialog>s opened with showModal()
     stay in the top layer and are not affected.
  5. The dialog needs to remain driven by the window. Even in the top layer, the
     dialog is still a descendant of the container in the DOM, so an @container
     rule could affect it. The dialog's rules (buttons side by side from 480px)
     stay in a window media query, on purpose, because the dialog takes up the
     window, not the block.
  6. The tests change in nature. Today they vary the window. They will start
     varying the block's width with the window fixed (and the reverse: narrow
     window with a wide block), to prove that the window no longer rules.

  What changes in the structure

  - SubscriptionCard.tsx: starts rendering the section with the title, the
    notices and the panels. Stops using useMediaQuery and starts using
    useContainerWidth.
  - New: shared/useContainerWidth.ts.
  - Removed: shared/useMediaQuery.ts.
  - SkipControl.tsx: the select, the notice and the button. The radios go away.
  - format.ts: nextChargeAfterSkip accepts N shipments.
  - CSS: the block as a container, columns and panel padding with @container, the
    lighter radius and background on the panels. The window media queries remain
    only on the dialog and on the page's outer spacing.
  - README: I update the paragraph about the screen's organization (select and
    container queries).

  Tests I change and ones I add

  - I change: the selector scenario (radios and arrows become a select), the
    touch targets, the Tab order, the order and width scenarios, and the dialog
    closing regression test.
  - I add:
    - The labels of the 6 options, with the dates computed independently.
    - Changing the select does not open a dialog.
    - The label for "2 shipments" equals the date obtained after two real skips
      in a row.
    - The block forced to 719px and to 720px.
    - The wide block with a narrow window (two columns) and the narrow block
      with a wide window (one column).
    - The DOM order follows the block, without a jump on the first render
      (measured per painted frame and also per mutation).

  1. Do I keep the year on the options when it is not the current one?

     transparent?
  3. Does the block's 720px threshold work?

● I explain each point with examples. I have not altered anything yet.

  1. The year on the select options

  Each option shows the date of the next charge after skipping that number of
  shipments. Since the subscription repeats every 30 days, skipping 6 shipments
  takes the date 180 days ahead, and it may fall in the following year.

  Today is October 29, 2026, and the next charge is on November 4. The options
  would look like this:

  ┌────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │        Without the year        │ With the year (only when it is not the │
  │                                │              current year)             │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 1 shipment · next charge Dec 4 │ 1 shipment · next charge Dec 4         │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2 shipments · next charge Jan  │ 2 shipments · next charge Jan 3, 2027  │
  │ 3                              │                                        │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 3 shipments · next charge Feb  │ 3 shipments · next charge Feb 2, 2027  │
  │ 2                              │                                        │
  ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ … up to 6                      │ … up to 6 (May 3, 2027)                │
  └────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Without the year, "Jan 3" does not say whether it is January of this year
  (already past) or of the next. In practice, whoever reads it understands that
  it is the next, but one more doubt remains, and the year costs only a few
  characters. With the year, option 1 stays short, because Dec 4 is still 2026.

  I recommend keeping the year on dates that are not in the current year.

  What I need from you: keep the year (my recommendation) or follow your
  example, with no year on any option?

  2. About "the block being white"

  It is not because of that, and my block is not white. The block that wraps
  everything would be transparent, without border or background, just an
  invisible grouping.

  You had asked for "no heavy card inside card". I interpreted that this block
  will be placed inside another white card (for example, a customer account
  page). In that case, the two panels, which today are white cards with very
  rounded corners, would end up as a white card inside a white card.

  That is why I proposed making the panels lighter:
  - Less rounded corners: from 24px to 14px.
  - Transparent subscription panel, with only a thin border. It works both on a
    white background and on today's cream background.
  - The next shipment panel keeps the pale-green background, which stays
    highlighted on any background.

  I understood your "if it is that then yes" as approval, but the condition is
  not quite what you imagined. So I only need to confirm: do I apply this
  lighter version of the panels?

  3. The 720px threshold

  It is the minimum width of the block, not of the browser window, from which
  the two columns appear:

  - Less than 720px of block width: one column, with the next shipment on top
    and the subscription below (the phone order).
  - 720px or more: two columns, with the subscription on the left and the next
    shipment on the right.

  Examples:
  - An 800px-wide block ends up in two columns.
  - A 500px block inside a 1400px window ends up in one column. This happens when
    it is in a sidebar or in a narrow column of the page.
  - A 700px block ends up in one column, even in a large window.

  I chose 720px because each column still ends up with about 350px, which gives
  about 310px usable inside the panel. The product lines, the select with texts
  like "2 shipments · next charge Jan 3, 2027" and the buttons fit without
  being cramped. It is also equivalent to what exists today, where the columns
  appear with the window at 768px, which gives a block of about 736px.

  If the number were smaller, say 600px, each column would have about 290px. The
  select's texts would be cut off and the product lines would be tight. If it
  were much larger, say 900px, the two columns would only appear on large
  screens.

  What I need from you: can I use 720px, or do you have a width in mind?

  4. "Go back to 1": it is about the dialog, not about the real skip

  The question is about the informational dialog, the one that says "Skipping
  several shipments isn't available yet". It is not about the real skip's
  confirmation.

  What happens today:
  1. The customer chooses, for example, "3 shipments" in the select.
  2. Clicks the "Skip next 3 shipments" button.
  3. The informational dialog opens, and nothing is sent to the API.
  4. The customer closes the dialog (Close button or Esc).
  5. The select goes back to "1 shipment", and the button goes back to saying
     "Skip next shipment".

  That takes the customer back to the only option that works. The alternative
  would be to leave "3 shipments" selected after closing. Then the customer
  would be left with a button that always opens the same notice, and would need
  to adjust the select again to do something useful.

  I recommend going back to 1, which is how it already works.

  What I need from you: go back to 1 when closing the dialog (recommended), or
  keep the choice?

  Summary of the answers I need

  1. Year on the select dates: keep it on those that are not the current year
     (recommended) or omit it?
  2. Lighter panels (14px corners and a transparent subscription panel): do I
     apply them?
  3. 720px threshold: does it work?
  4. When closing the informational dialog: go back to 1 (recommended) or keep
     the choice?

  I incorporated your four answers. Before putting the plan together, I measured

     1 stays "Dec 4" and options 2 to 6 carry "2027".

     so it does not become a heavy card inside a card.
  3. Container threshold: you approved 720px, but I recommend 768px
     (explanation below).

  1. Native select from 1 to 6 shipments

  - A "Shipments to skip" label linked to a native <select>, 48px tall and with
    a 14px font, the same as Part B's select.
  - The options are "N shipment(s) · next charge DATE":

  ┌────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Option │                 Text                  │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 1      │ 1 shipment · next charge Dec 4        │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2      │ 2 shipments · next charge Jan 3, 2027 │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 3      │ 3 shipments · next charge Feb 2, 2027 │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 4      │ 4 shipments · next charge Mar 4, 2027 │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 5      │ 5 shipments · next charge Apr 3, 2027 │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 6      │ 6 shipments · next charge May 3, 2027 │
  └────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

  - The calculation is nextChargeAt + N × intervalDays × 24h in milliseconds,
    the same as the mock's skip. nextChargeAfterSkip starts accepting N, and
    with N = 1 the confirmation dialog does not change. This also holds for
    daylight saving time passages: the calculation is exact in milliseconds, so
    the local time may change by 1h, but the date only changes near midnight.
  - Fixed notice: "For now, shipments can only be skipped one at a time." stays
    visible and describes the select and the button.
  - Button: the label follows ("Skip next shipment" with 1 and "Skip next N
    shipments" with 2 or more). With 1 it is the real skip, with confirmation
    and API. With 2 or more, the click on the button opens the informational
    dialog, without calling the API. Changing the select never opens a dialog.
    On closing the dialog, the choice goes back to 1.

  2. The white block and the light panels

  Structure: a white and visible <section aria-labelledby>, inside the <main>,
  containing the title (h1), the notices and the two panels. The dialogs stay
  outside it. Inside there is a second element with the padding, because a
  container cannot apply to itself a rule of its own container.

  Proposed measurements:

  ┌──────────────────┬───────────────────┬───────────────┬─────────────────┐
  │                  │ Narrow window     │ Block from 480│ Block from 768px│
  │                  │     (phone)       │    to 767px   │                 │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Page margin      │ 12px              │ 16px          │ 16px            │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Block: border,   │ 1px, 24px         │ same          │ same            │
  │ radius           │                   │               │                 │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Block's inner    │ 16px              │ 20px          │ 24px            │
  │ padding          │                   │               │                 │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Panels: radius   │ 14px, thin 1px    │ same          │ same            │
  │ and border       │                   │               │                 │
  ├──────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Panel padding    │ 16px              │ 16px          │ 20px            │
  └──────────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────┴─────────────────┘

  Light panels: no shadow. The subscription panel is transparent, with only the
  thin border (over the block's white). The next shipment panel keeps the
  pale-green background, which highlights it without weight. The panels' corners
  stay at 14px, versus the block's 24px.

  3. Container queries and width observer

  - The block becomes a container (container-type: inline-size). The two columns
    and the paddings start depending on the block's inner width (discounting the
    borders), with @container. The window no longer rules over this.
  - A useContainerWidth hook uses ResizeObserver to decide the order in the DOM.
    The initial measurement is synchronous (useLayoutEffect), before painting,
    and the panels are only rendered after it. The title and the notices appear
    first. That way the DOM never goes through a wrong order and the "the order
    never changes" test remains valid.
  - On width changes, the callback commits the update synchronously (flushSync),
    so there is no frame with new columns and old order.
  - The threshold lives in two places (CSS and the hook), with a comment in both
    and a test that forces the block to just below and just above it, checking
    that columns and order change together.
  - The dialog's rules (buttons side by side from 480px) stay in a window media
    query, on purpose. The dialog takes up the window, and an @container could
    affect it even in the top layer, since it is a descendant of the container.
  - useMediaQuery stops being used and I remove it.

  What the measurement showed, and what that changes

  I measured the text of the six options in Inter 500. The longest is 260px at
  14px (278px at 15px and 297px at 16px). Adding the left padding (12px) and the
  chevron zone (≈32px), the select needs ≈304px of width to show everything.

  With the white block, the select's usable space is smaller than I had said
  before, because now there is page margin, border and block padding, and panel
  border and padding:

  ┌──────────────────────────┬───────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
  │        Situation         │ Usable width of   │ Does the longest option  │
  │                          │    the select     │      (304px) fit?        │
  ├──────────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ 375px window (one        │ ≈ 283px           │ No: cuts ≈ 21px, the end │
  │ column)                  │                   │  of the year             │
  ├──────────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ Two columns with the     │ ≈ 282px           │ No: cuts ≈ 22px          │
  │ block at 720px           │                   │                          │
  ├──────────────────────────┼───────────────────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ Two columns with the     │ ≈ 314px           │ Yes, with 10px of slack  │
  │ block at 768px           │                   │                          │
  └──────────────────────────┴───────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

  That is why I recommend 768px instead of 720px. The 720px you approved assumed
  panels without the block around them. With 768px the select fits entirely in
  two columns. Between 720 and 767px the block stays in one column, which loses
  nothing.

  On the phone (375px) the cut has no cheap solution. For the longest option to
  fit entirely it would take 8px margins and 12px paddings, which is tight. Two
  alternatives:
  - A (recommended): accept that, in the closed select at ~375px, the end of the
    year on options 2 to 6 appears with an ellipsis ("…May 3, 20…"). The day and
    the month stay visible, and the native select's open list usually shows the
    whole text (I did not test on a real device).
  - B: tighten margins and paddings so everything fits, at the cost of a more
    squeezed block.

  - Text: "Your next charge would be on DATE." For example, "Your next charge would
    be on Jan 3, 2027." and, with 1, "Your next charge would be on Dec 4." It
    follows the rule: "charge" in the date sentence, "shipment" in the options.
  - Behavior: it follows the select and uses the same date as the chosen option.
  - Order on screen: label, select, support line, the fixed notice "For now,
    shipments can only be skipped one at a time." and the button. The support
    line uses the body text color, because it is the consequence of the choice.
    The notice stays in the secondary color.
  - Font: 13px, the same as the notice. I measured the longest line at 266px,
    which fits on one line even in the tightest cases (phone and two columns at
    the threshold).
  - Accessibility: the line and the notice describe the select
    (aria-describedby), but it is not a live region. When the select changes, the
    screen reader already announces the new option, which includes the date, and
    a live region would repeat the information.

  3. The threshold stays at 720px, and alternatives A and B are dropped

  With the short text, the select fits in the two cases where space was missing
  before. The formula is the same as before: the select needs the text plus 44px
  (left padding and the chevron zone).

  ┌─────────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────┬────────────────┐
  │                 │ Usable width │  Needs (longest      │                │
  │    Situation    │  of select   │  option) at 14px /   │     Fits?      │
  │                 │              │       at 16px        │                │
  ├─────────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ 375px window    │              │                      │ Yes, with 62px │
  │ (one column)    │ ≈ 283px      │ 221px / 246px        │ / 37px of      │
  │                 │              │                      │ slack          │
  ├─────────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Two columns,    │              │                      │ Yes, with 61px │
  │ block at 720px  │ ≈ 282px      │ 221px / 246px        │ / 36px of      │
  │                 │              │                      │ slack          │
  └─────────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────────┴────────────────┘

  A small new suggestion: a 16px font on the select, instead of 14px. iOS zooms
  automatically when focusing a field with a font smaller than 16px, and that
  would disorganize the screen on the phone. With 16px the select still fits in
  both cases, as the table shows. Part B uses 14px because of the design
  reference, but here there is no such restriction. If you prefer 14px, it is one
  line.

  4. Tests

  - I replace the truncation check with one that the longest option fits in the
    select. It becomes a real assertion, both at 375px and with the block at
    720px in two columns, using the text width measured by canvas against the
    select's usable width.
  - Text of the options: the 6 options in the new format, with the dates computed
    independently.
  - Support line:
    - The text matches each option's date, and changes when the select changes.
    - It stays on one line at 375px and at the two-column threshold.
    - The select references it through aria-describedby.
  - Kept: changing the select never opens a dialog; the date for "2 shipments"
    equals the one obtained after two real skips in a row; on closing the
    informational dialog the choice goes back to 1.

  5. What stays the same as the approved plan

  Everything else remains: the visible white block with the measurements of
  section 2, the light panels, the container queries with the 720px threshold,
  the observer with synchronous measurement before painting and flushSync on
  changes, the removal of useMediaQuery, the dialog rules in a window media
  query, the build order (with a commit and push per step) and the README
  update.

  What I need from you

  1. Select font at 16px (recommended, because of iOS zoom) or 14px?
  2. Can I proceed with the plan as it is?

  I await your confirmation before touching any file.

  typecheck at each step. At the end, run the test battery and list what
  passed, what
  failed and what you are least sure about.

● Bash(SP="C:/Users/danie/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/c--Nimue-Digital-Railcom-nat

● I implemented the four steps and the battery passed: 252 checks passing and 0
  failing, in two full runs in a row. The typecheck passed at every step, and
  each step became a commit with a push, without co-authorship. The repository
  is clean.

  What was done

  1. White block as a container.
     - Title, notices and panels sit inside a white section, and the columns
       depend on the block's width through @container (720px).
     - A hook with ResizeObserver decides the order of the panels. The initial
       measurement is synchronous before painting, and the panels only render
       after it.
     - useMediaQuery was removed.
  2. Native select from 1 to 6, at 16px, with options N shipment(s) · DATE and
     the line "Your next charge would be on DATE.". The fixed notice stays
     visible.
  3. Light panels, with 14px corners, the subscription one without a background,
     and the README updated.
  4. Tests updated and expanded.

  What passed

  - Select: the 6 options in the new format, with dates computed independently.
    - Changing the select never opens a dialog nor calls the API.
    - The date for "2 shipments" equals the one obtained after two real skips in
      a row, and the one for "1 shipment" equals that of the confirmation
      dialog.
    - The longest option fits without an ellipsis: 202px in 229px on the phone
      and 202px in 228px with the block at the 720px threshold.
    - The support line stays on a single line in those two cases and describes
      the select through aria-describedby.
  - Independence from the window: with the window fixed at 1400px, the block at
    719px is in 1 column and at 720px in 2, and the order changes with it. With
    the window at 375px and the block forced to 900px, the layout is in 2
    columns. The select's state survives all the switches.
  - Visual: the block is white, with a border and a 24px radius, and title,
    notices and panels are inside it. The panels have no shadow and have 14px
    corners. Paddings and margins match the approved table.
  - Order without a jump: the panels' order does not change from the first
    render to the loaded page, in the three fixtures and at both widths.
  - Regression: the real skip, the 409, the 400 and the 404, the 500 inside the
    dialog, the reload failure, the double click, the out-of-order responses,
    the focus, the Tab order and the closing of the dialogs keep passing.

  What failed, and what it was

  In the first full round it was 223 passing and 29 failing. They were two real
  defects of mine and one test adjustment:
  - CSS class collision (real):
    - The CSS had two .block rules, the new container's and the old one for the
      paused and cancelled notices. In a CSS Module they merge.
    - The block ended up with 14px corners instead of 24px and gained an extra
      16px of padding. That is why the content ended up 34px narrower than
      designed, the longest option did not fit on the phone and the support line
      wrapped.
    - The old class became emptyState, and I confirmed there are no more
      duplicated rules.
  - flushSync during rendering (real): the initial measurement called flushSync
    while React was rendering, and React logs that as an error in the console.
    The initial measurement now uses a plain setState, which inside a
    useLayoutEffect is applied before painting. flushSync stayed only in the
    observer.
  - Count of section: the test expected 2 and now it is 3 (the block and the two
    panels).

  Less certain

  - I only tested in Chrome. The container query, the ResizeObserver with
    flushSync and the native select were not verified in Firefox or Safari.
  - iOS: the choice of 16px is to avoid iOS zoom. I did not test it on a real
    device, so the effect is inferred.
  - Open select: I only checked the closed select's text. The look of the open
    list on the phone, and whether it shows the whole text, was not tested.
  - Widths close to the threshold: I tested exactly 719px and 720px and
    variations between them. The behavior while dragging the block continuously
    was not observed, only discrete switches.
  - Wide block in one column: between 480 and 719px of block width, the panels
    take up the full width, and I did not visually review whether that ends up
    too stretched. It is a consequence of the width no longer being limited to
    560px.
  - Two places for the 720px threshold: the CSS and the TypeScript. The test only
    covers exactly 719/720, so a future change in just one of them would be
    caught, but not by a build warning.
  - Dates: the tests compute the expected date with the current time and I only
    tested in my time zone. A daylight saving change in the middle of the
    180-day path (6 shipments) may shift the local time by 1h.
  - Screen readers and a real phone: I only checked the structure (labels,
    aria-describedby, regions). I did not listen with NVDA or VoiceOver, and the
    375px is emulation.
  - Part B: it keeps working, but I did not rerun the pixel comparison with the
    reference.
  - Tests outside the repository: they remain in a temporary folder. I can add
    them, with a development dependency.

> if I test the cancel and skip shipment buttons and even the add to cart
  button, what is the expected behavior?

● Part A (/?part=a&fixture=active)

  Cancel subscription

  It appears on active and paused subscriptions, and does not appear on the
  cancelled one.
  - On click: it opens a "Cancellation isn't available yet" dialog. The text, on
    an active subscription, is: "We can't cancel a subscription from this page
    yet. Nothing was changed: your subscription is active and your next charge is
    still on Wed, Nov 4." On paused, it ends with "Your subscription hasn't
    changed."
  - Focus: focus goes to the only button, "Close". Esc, a click on the backdrop
    and "Close" close it, and focus returns to the "Cancel subscription" link.
  - What does not happen: no API call and no change on the screen.

  Skip next shipment (with 1 selected, which is the default)

  It opens the "Skip your next shipment?" confirmation, with the sentence "Your
  $101.87 charge on Wed, Nov 4 will move to Fri, Dec 4." and "Your subscription
  stays active." Focus starts on "Keep my shipment". "Keep", Esc and a click on
  the backdrop close it without doing anything. "Yes, skip shipment" calls the
  API and changes the button to "Skipping…" (0.4 to 2.5s). During that wait,
  Esc, a click on the backdrop and "Keep" do not close the dialog, and a second
  click does not send a second call.

  The result depends on the API's response:

  Result: Success
  What you see: The dialog closes. "Shipment skipped" appears with "Your $101.87
  charge is now on Fri, Dec 4 (was Wed, Nov 4)." The panel's date changes and
  focus returns to the button. You can skip again, always with confirmation
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Result: 409 (the subscription changed elsewhere)
  What you see: The dialog closes. Red notice "Your subscription changed… We've
  refreshed the details below. Check them and skip again if you still want to."
  The date does not change, the data is reloaded ("Refreshing…"), and the skip
  is never repeated on its own
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Result: 500 or network error
  What you see: The dialog stays open, with "Something went wrong. Your shipment
  wasn't skipped." and the "Try again" button, which is a click of yours
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Result: 400
  What you see: Notice "This subscription can't skip a shipment right now…" and
  reload
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Result: 404
  What you see: Notice "We couldn't find this subscription." with a "Reload"
  button
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Result: Reload fails after a 409
  What you see: The notice says that the skip was not done and that the reload
  failed. The skip button stays disabled until the notice's "Try again" works

  By default the mock rejects about 1 in every 3 skips with a 409. To test in a
  predictable way, use &conflict=never (always success) or &conflict=always
  (always 409). The mock's state stays in the page's memory, so reloading the
  page resets everything.

  Skip with 2 to 6 shipments

  Choosing in the select opens nothing. It only changes the button's label ("Skip
  next 3 shipments") and the support line ("Your next charge would be on Feb 2,
  2027."). Clicking the button opens the "Skipping several shipments isn't
  available yet" dialog, without calling the API. On closing (Close button or
  Esc), the select goes back to 1.

  Paused and cancelled

  No skip button appears (&fixture=paused and &fixture=cancelled).

  Part B (/?part=b): Add to cart

  It does nothing, and that is what the brief asks for ("adding to the cart
  doesn't need to do anything"). Clicking does not open a dialog, does not show a
  notice and does not change any state, and there is no visual click feedback
  beyond the focus ring on the keyboard. The button only shows the current total
  ("Add to cart · $25.46"), which follows the purchase option and the quantity.

  If you want some feedback when clicking Add to cart (for example, a quick
  confirmation), it is a product decision that I did not make on my own.

> if I click on yes, skip my shipment, what is the expected behavior?

● The button is called "Yes, skip shipment". When you click it, the following
  happens, in order:

  1. Right away (without waiting for the API):
     - The button changes to "Skipping…" and becomes inert, and the click is
       accepted only once, so a double click does not send two calls.
     - "Keep my shipment", Esc and a click on the backdrop stop closing the
       dialog, because closing would not cancel the request and you would lose
       the result.
     - Focus stays on the button.
  2. Wait: 0.4 to 2.5s, which is the mock's latency. Nothing on the background
     screen changes in that time.
  3. API response, which can be:

  Result: Success (about 2 in 3 by default, always with &conflict=never)
  What you see: The dialog closes. The "Shipment skipped" notice appears with
  "Your $101.87 charge is now on Fri, Dec 4 (was Wed, Nov 4)." The big date in
  the next shipment panel changes to Dec 4. Focus returns to the "Skip next
  shipment" button
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Result: 409 (about 1 in 3 by default, always with &conflict=always)
  What you see: The dialog closes. Red notice "Your subscription changed…
  We've refreshed the details below. Check them and skip again if you still
  want to." The date does not change, and the skip is not repeated on its own
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Result: 500 or network error
  What you see: The dialog stays open, with "Something went wrong. Your
  shipment wasn't skipped." The button changes to "Try again", and the skip is
  only redone if you click again
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Result: 400 or 404
  What you see: The dialog closes with a notice explaining what happened (on the
  404, with
  a "Reload" button)

  After success, what else changes on the screen:
  - The select recalculates all the options from the new date. For example, "1
    shipment" starts showing the date after the new one, and the support line
    follows.
  - The select stays on "1".
  - You can skip again. The confirmation opens again, now with "Fri, Dec 4" as
    the starting date, and the second skip works without giving a 409, because
    the app now uses the new version of the subscription.
  - The success notice stays on screen until you open the skip dialog again.

  To test in a predictable way, open /?part=a&fixture=active&conflict=never
  (always success) or &conflict=always (always 409). The mock's state stays in
  the page's memory, so reloading the page resets the date.

> if I refresh does it go back to how it was before?

● Yes, it goes back to how it was before.

  The API mock keeps the state in the page's own memory. On reloading (F5), the
  module is recreated and the subscription goes back to the original data: the
  next charge 6 days from now, the same total and the same date. The skip you
  did disappears, and the "Shipment skipped" notice too.

  This applies to the test's mock. In a real API, the skip would be saved on the
  server, and on reloading the page the new date would still be there.

  Two details:
  - The starting date is relative to now. The fixtures compute "today + 6 days"
    at the moment the page loads. After reloading, the date may be different
    from the previous one if a day or more has passed.
  - Switching tabs or going back with the browser button also reloads the mock,
    because the page is mounted again. The state only persists while you do not
    reload.

> I skipped twice and this message appeared
  Your subscription changed

  It was updated somewhere else, so we didn't skip your shipment. We've
  refreshed the details below. Check them and skip again if you still want to.

  can you explain what happened?

● It was the 409 error, the rejection for "the subscription changed elsewhere".
  That is the mock's expected behavior, not a defect of the screen.

  Why it appeared

  The API mock simulates that conflict on purpose. Without the &conflict
  parameter in the URL, in about 1 in every 3 skip requests it "alters the
  subscription elsewhere" before processing. In practice it updates the
  subscription's version (updatedAt). Since the screen sent the version it had
  loaded, the server sees that it no longer matches and refuses with a 409, so as
  not to overwrite a change the customer did not see.

  Skipping twice, the chance of at least one conflict is about 55%, so it is
  quite common.

  What the screen did

  - It did not skip the shipment. The next charge's date stays the same as before
    that click.
  - It reloaded the data ("Refreshing…"), so the screen now has the
    subscription's new version.
  - It did not try again on its own. The decision to repeat is yours, because the
    data may have changed.

  How to know what happened in your two attempts

  - If the notice appeared after the second attempt, the first one worked. The
    date should have advanced once, 30 days.
  - If it appeared right after the first, neither worked, and the date is the
    original.

  Just look at the big date in the next shipment panel.

  What to do now

  Click "Skip next shipment" again and confirm. Since the screen already has the
  current version, that new attempt only fails again if the mock's draw hits
  again.

  To test without a draw

  - &conflict=never in the URL: the skip always works.
  - &conflict=always: the skip always gives a 409, useful for seeing that message
    on purpose.

  For example, /?part=a&fixture=active&conflict=never.

  In a real API, the same message would appear if the customer had the page open
  in two places, or if someone from support altered the subscription after they
  loaded it.

> I clicked again and now it went through,
  but what is the point of doing this? is it just to test a random impediment?

● Yes, the randomness is artificial. What it represents, however, is a real
  situation, and the test's brief asks for it: "The API sometimes rejects a skip
  because the subscription changed elsewhere. The customer must always know what
  happened and what to do." The 1-in-3 draw only exists so that you can see that
  path working.

  What the conflict protects in real life

  The API compares the version of the subscription that the screen loaded with
  the current version on the server. If they do not match, it refuses. That
  prevents an outdated screen from making a decision about data that has already
  changed. Examples:
  - The subscription was touched elsewhere. The customer left the page open on
    the laptop and already skipped the shipment on the phone. Skipping again
    would push the charge back one more month without them noticing.
  - The status changed. Support paused or cancelled the subscription while the
    screen was open. A skip under those conditions should not happen.
  - An automatic process altered the subscription. For example, a rescheduling
    after a failed charge.

  In all of those cases, the right thing is to refuse, show the current situation
  and let the customer decide again. That is what the screen does: it does not
  skip, it reloads the data and it does not repeat on its own, because, after
  reloading, the customer may no longer want to skip.

  Why this matters for the screen

  Without this care, the screen could:
  - say "Shipment skipped" when nothing happened;
  - show a generic error, leaving the customer not knowing whether the shipment
    was skipped or not;
  - try again on its own and skip a shipment they did not want.

  That is why the message says what happened ("It was updated somewhere else, so
  we didn't skip your shipment"), what was done ("We've refreshed the details
  below") and what to do now ("skip again if you still want to").

  In a real API that conflict would be rare, not 1 in 3. In the test it appears
  often on purpose, so that whoever evaluates can see how the screen behaves when
  something goes wrong.

> on the product screen the add to cart button does not work, is that really how
  it should be, correct, only changing the purchase type subscription or
  one-time purchase work

● Yes, that is right. The brief says: "Selecting a purchase option and changing
  the quantity should work; adding to the cart doesn't need to do anything." The
  "Add to cart" button only shows the current total and does nothing else.

  What works on the product screen is not only the choice between subscription
  and one-time purchase:
  - Purchase type: it changes the button's price, from $25.46 on the subscription
    to $29.95 on the one-time purchase. On the one-time purchase the renewal text
    disappears.
  - Quantity: it goes from 1 to 99, with the − and + buttons. The button's total
    follows (for example, 2 × $25.46 = $50.92).
  - Delivery frequency (30, 60 or 90 days): it only appears on the subscription
    and changes the renewal text ("renews every 60 days at…"). The price does
    not change with it.

  If you want the button to give some feedback when clicked, like a quick
  confirmation, I can propose that. I did not do it on my own, because the brief
  explicitly says it is not needed.

> the manage my subscription screen takes a while to show the values, why does
  this happen?

● That is the API mock's simulated latency, and not a problem with the screen.

  In src/api/mockApi.ts, the wait function is 400 + Math.random() * 2100, that
  is, between 0.4 and 2.5 random seconds on each call. That applies to loading
  the subscription and to the skip. The brief asks for that on purpose: "The API
  is sometimes slow", so that the screen shows how it behaves while waiting.

  What the screen does in that time

  - It shows a skeleton (gray shapes in place of the panels), in the same order of
    the columns the screen will have. Whoever uses a screen reader hears
    "Loading your subscription…".
  - After the data arrives, the values and the date appear all at once.

  Why it sometimes seems longer

  - In development, the loading happens twice. React (StrictMode) runs the effect
    twice, so there are two calls to the mock. The first is discarded and the
    second counts, but that does not add up the time: the two waits run in
    parallel, and what you wait for is the second call's.
  - Since the time is drawn, in some loads you wait close to 0.4s and in others
    close to 2.5s.

  In production that would not happen in this way: the real API would have the
  latency of the network and the server, and there would be no StrictMode
  duplicating the call.

  If you want to test with a shorter or longer time, or see the skeleton for
  longer, I can show how to fix the latency only in the test, without changing
  the project's mock. I will not alter mockApi.ts, because it is part of what
  the test provides.

> according to the brief, did we reach all the test objectives, correct?

● Almost everything, but before answering I check two points that I had not yet
  verified: whether the production build works (it is what Vercel runs) and
  whether the repository is public.

  Part B: met with one caveat

  The option selection and the quantity work, and the comparison with the
  reference gave an average pixel difference of 1.09 out of 255. Two elements
  ended up 1 to 2 device px (less than 1 CSS px) off: the "VITAMINS" and the
  underline of "2,316 reviews". I would not say "pixel-perfect" without that
  caveat. I also did not rerun the comparison after the later changes, but they
  did not touch any of Part B's styles.

● The deploy is correct and it is the latest version of the code. I verified the
  published site end to end, with the same method as the local tests.

  Result

  - Availability: Part B, Part A and product.jpg respond 200.
  - Part B: the title, the photo, the total and the renewal text work, and
    quantity 2 gives $50.92. One-time purchase shows $29.95, and 90 days appears
    in the renewal text.
  - Fidelity to the reference: the average pixel difference is 1.186 out of 255,
    identical to the local version's with the same comparison. The published
    site renders exactly like the local one.
  - Part A (375px and 1200px):
    - The card appears after the simulated latency, with the right order of the
      panels: the next shipment on top on the phone and the subscription on the
      left on desktop.
    - The select has the 6 options in the final format, and there is no
      horizontal scroll.
    - A real end-to-end skip worked: it opened the confirmation with focus on
      "Keep my shipment", and then showed "Shipment skipped" with "(was …)".
  - Fixtures: paused, cancelled and error show the right messages and no skip
    button. With conflict=always, the 409 is explained.
  - Latest version: in the published version, the block has a 24px radius, the
    select uses 16px, the longest option fits (202px in 229px on the phone, and
    202px in 228px with the block at 720px), and there are no console errors.
    That confirms the CSS collision fix is live. The build file names differ
    from those of my local build, but that did not show any difference in
    behavior.

  What appeared and is not a real problem

  - A 404 in Part B's console: it is /favicon.ico. The project has no favicon,
    and Vercel responds 404. It is harmless, but the 404 is visible in the
    browser's tools. If you want, I can add a simple favicon, but it is not
    required by the brief.
  - Two of my checks failed, and they were the test's:
    - The font check was fragile. Fraunces and Inter really do load (Google
      Fonts responded 200 and the visual comparison matches).
    - The "no problems" test treated the favicon's 404 as an error.

  What is still missing for the delivery

  The deploy covers the "live link", but the form also asks for the link to the
  conversation with the AI: a share link or the chat exported into the
  repository, stating the path. Only you can do that.

  I can add the favicon, or export a summary of the work to the repository, if it
